1. **Default text**: Buttons have "Click" as default text instead of being empty
2. **Icon alignment**: When buttons have no text, icons should be centered but remain left-aligned

### 5.2 Problem Location

- Line 39: Default `msg="Click"` should be `msg=""`
- Lines 70-84: `_set_gliph` method doesn't handle icon alignment
- Lines 86-94: `_set_text` method doesn't handle icon alignment

- Default buttons show "Click" text
- Empty msg buttons still have left-aligned icons
- Icon alignment logic is missing

- Ensure icon alignment logic is consistent

3. Add logic in `_set_text` to handle icon alignment based on text content

    18	
    19	    Args:
    20	        msg (str, optional): the text to display in the btn

    25	
    26	    .. deprecated:: 2.13
    27	        ``text`` and ``icon`` will be replaced by ``msg`` and<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    __shared__ float smem[THREADS_PER_BLOCK];
    
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    const int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    
    const float* row_x = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* row_y = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    // Initialize for max reduction
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    
    // Vectorized load and max computation
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, __ldg(row_x + i));
    }
    
    // Warp-level reduction (no divergent branches)
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        float other = __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, offset);
        thread_max = max(thread_max, other);
    }
    
    // First thread in each warp writes to shared memory
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        smem[warp_id] = thread_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction in first warp
    if (tid < (THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE)) {
        thread_max = smem[tid];
    }
    
    if (tid < WARP_SIZE) {
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE)/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
            float other = __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, offset);
            thread_max = max(thread_max, other);
        }
    }
    
    // Broadcast max value to all threads
    float max_val = __shfl_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, 0);
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Compute exp and sum
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        float val = __expf(__ldg(row_x + i) - max_val);
        row_y[i] = val;  // Store intermediate result
        thread_sum += val;
    }
    
    // Warp-level sum reduction
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        thread_sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, offset);
    }
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        smem[warp_id] = thread_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    if (tid < (THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE)) {
        thread_sum = smem[tid];
    }
    
    if (tid < WARP_SIZE) {
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE)/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
            thread_sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, offset);
        }
    }
    
    float sum_val = __shfl_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, 0);
    
    // Normalize with vectorized operations
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        row_y[i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size);
    dim3 block(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, block>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

__device__ __forceinline__ float warp_reduce_max(float val) {
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2)
        val = max(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset));
    return val;
}

__device__ __forceinline__ float warp_reduce_sum(float val) {
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2)
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset);
    return val;
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, 
                             float* __restrict__ y, 
                             const int num_features) {
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    const int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    const int warps_per_block = THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE;
    
    // Calculate optimal stride based on feature size
    const int stride = (num_features + THREADS_PER_BLOCK - 1) / THREADS_PER_BLOCK * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;
    
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    __shared__ float warp_maxes[warps_per_block];
    __shared__ float warp_sums[warps_per_block];

    // Find max value
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        if (i < num_features) {
            thread_max = max(thread_max, __ldg(&x_row[i]));
        }
    }
    
    // Warp-level reduction
    float warp_max = warp_reduce_max(thread_max);
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_maxes[warp_id] = warp_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction for max
    if (tid < warps_per_block) {
        float block_max = warp_maxes[tid];
        block_max = warp_reduce_max(block_max);
        if (tid == 0) {
            warp_maxes[0] = block_max;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    const float max_val = warp_maxes[0];
    
    // Compute exp and sum
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        if (i < num_features) {
            const float val = __expf(__ldg(&x_row[i]) - max_val);
            y_row[i] = val;
            thread_sum += val;
        }
    }
    
    // Warp-level reduction for sum
    float warp_sum = warp_reduce_sum(thread_sum);
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_sums[warp_id] = warp_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction for sum
    if (tid < warps_per_block) {
        float block_sum = warp_sums[tid];
        block_sum = warp_reduce_sum(block_sum);
        if (tid == 0) {
            warp_sums[0] = block_sum;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    const float sum_val = warp_sums[0];
    
    // Normalize with optimized stride
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        if (i < num_features) {
            y_row[i] /= sum_val;
        }
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size);
    dim3 block(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, block>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

Alternatively, let me check with another approach. Since F_n = (φ^n - ψ^n)/√5, as before, then F_n² = (φ^{2n} + ψ^{2n} - 2(-1)^n)/5.

Therefore, the sum S = ∑_{n=1}^\infty n F_n² /3^n = (1/5)∑_{n=1}^\infty n [φ^{2n} + ψ^{2n} - 2(-1)^n ] /3^n.

Which can be written as (1/5)[ ∑_{n=1}^\infty n (φ²/3)^n + ∑_{n=1}^\infty n (ψ²/3)^n - 2 ∑_{n=1}^\infty n (-1/3)^n ]

Each of these sums is a standard arithmetic-geometric series.

Recall that ∑_{n=1}^\infty n r^n = r/(1 - r)^2, for |r| <1.

So let's compute each term:

First term: ∑_{n=1}^\infty n (φ²/3)^n = (φ²/3)/(1 - φ²/3)^2

Second term: ∑_{n=1}^\infty n (ψ²/3)^n = (ψ²/3)/(1 - ψ²/3)^2

Third term: -2 ∑_{n=1}^\infty n (-1/3)^n = -2 * [ (-1/3) / (1 +1/3)^2 ] = -2 * [ (-1/3) / (16/9) ] = -2 * (-3/16) = 6/16=3/8

Therefore, S=(1/5)[ S1 + S2 + 3/8 ], where S1 = (φ²/3)/(1 - φ²/3)^2 and S2 = (ψ²/3)/(1 - ψ²/3)^2.

Compute S1:

Recall φ² = (3 +√5)/2

So φ²/3 = (3 +√5)/6

1 - φ²/3 =1 - (3 +√5)/6= (6 -3 -√5)/6=(3 -√5)/6

Thus, (1 - φ²/3)^2= (3 -√5)^2 /36=(9 -6√5 +5)/36=(14 -6√5)/36=(7 -3√5)/18

Therefore, S1= [ (3 +√5)/6 ] / [ (7 -3√5)/18 ]= [ (3 +√5)/6 ] * [18/(7 -3√5) ]= [ (3 +√5)*3 ] / (7 -3√5 )

Multiply numerator and denominator by (7 +3√5) to rationalize:

Numerator:3(3 +√5)(7 +3√5)

Denominator:(7 -3√5)(7 +3√5)=49 - (3√5)^2=49 -45=4

Compute numerator:

3[3*7 +3*3√5 +√5*7 +√5*3√5] =3[21 +9√5 +7√5 +15]

=3[21 +16√5 +15]

=3[36 +16√5]

=108 +48√5

Thus, S1=(108 +48√5)/4=27 +12√5

Similarly, compute S2.

ψ² = (3 -√5)/2

ψ²/3=(3 -√5)/6

1 - ψ²/3=1 - (3 -√5)/6=(6 -3 +√5)/6=(3 +√5)/6

Thus, (1 -ψ²/3)^2=(3 +√5)^2 /36=(9 +6√5 +5)/36=(14 +6√5)/36=(7 +3√5)/18

So S2= [ (3 -√5)/6 ] / [ (7 +3√5)/18 ]= [ (3 -√5)/6 ] * [18/(7 +3√5) ]= [ (3 -√5)*3 ] / (7 +3√5 )

Again, rationalize denominator by multiplying numerator and denominator by (7 -3√5):

Numerator:3(3 -√5)(7 -3√5)

Denominator:(7 +3√5)(7 -3√5)=49 -45=4

Compute numerator:

3[3*7 +3*(-3√5) -√5*7 +√5*3√5]

=3[21 -9√5 -7√5 +15]

=3[21 +15 -16√5]

=3[36 -16√5]

=108 -48√5

Thus, S2=(108 -48√5)/4=27 -12√5

Therefore, S1 + S2= (27 +12√5)+(27 -12√5)=54

Then, S=(1/5)[54 +3/8]=(1/5)*(54.375)=54.375/5=10.875=87/8.

Yes, that confirms the previous result. Therefore, the answer is 87/8.

</think>To evaluate the infinite sum \(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{n F_n^2}{3^n}\), where \(F_n\) is the \(n\)-th Fibonacci number, we will use generating functions and properties of Fibonacci numbers.

### Step 1: Generating Function for \(F_n^2\)
The generating function for \(F_n^2\) is given by:
\[
G(x) = \sum_{n=1}^{\infty} F_n^2 x^n = \frac{x(1 - x)}{(x^2 - 3x + 1)(x + 1)}
\]

### Step 2: Derivative of the Generating Function
To find the sum involving \(n F_n^2 x^n\), we take the derivative of \(G(x)\) and multiply by \(x\):
\[
x G'(x) = \sum_{n=1}^{\infty} n F_n^2 x^n
\]

\[
G(x) = \frac{x(1 - x)}{(x^2 - 3x + 1)(x + 1)}
\]

Using the quotient rule:
\[
G'(x) = \frac{N'(x)D(x) - N(x)D'(x)}{D(x)^2}

\[

   5.2<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// CUDA kernel declaration
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features);

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim((num_features + THREADS_PER_BLOCK - 1) / THREADS_PER_BLOCK, batch_size);

    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.y;
    int tid = threadIdx.x + blockIdx.x * blockDim.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x * gridDim.x;

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = x[batch_idx * num_features + i];
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }

    if (threadIdx.x == 0) {
        sdata[threadIdx.x] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = blockDim.x / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (threadIdx.x < s) {
            sdata[threadIdx.x] = max(sdata[threadIdx.x], sdata[threadIdx.x + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x[batch_idx * num_features + i] - max_val);
        y[batch_idx * num_features + i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    if (threadIdx.x == 0) {
        sdata[threadIdx.x] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = blockDim.x / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (threadIdx.x < s) {
            sdata[threadIdx.x] += sdata[threadIdx.x + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y[batch_idx * num_features + i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

- Best practice: Icon-only buttons should not have default text
- Convention: Empty strings should not affect layout alignment

### 1.2 Technical Details
- **Class**: `sepal_ui.Btn` (imported as `sw.Btn`)
- **Parameters**: `icon=True`, `gliph="mdi-plus"`, `msg`, `children`
- **Error**: Default text "click" is added automatically
- **Layout issue**: Empty text causes icon misalignment

### 1.3 Problem Explanation
The `sepal_ui.Btn` class automatically adds default text "click" when no message is provided. When users try to create icon-only buttons by setting empty strings, the icon alignment becomes incorrect (left-aligned instead of centered).

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create `sw.Btn(icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")` → Shows "click" text (undesired)
2. Create `sw.Btn(children=[""], icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")` → Icon misaligned
3. Create `sw.Btn(msg="", icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")` → Icon misaligned

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Icon-only buttons should not have text content
- Empty text should not affect layout
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Make minimal changes to existing code

    20<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// CUDA kernel declaration
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features);

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim((batch_size + THREADS_PER_BLOCK - 1) / THREADS_PER_BLOCK);

    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }

    sdata[tid] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];

    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
        float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];

    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}

A circle of radius \( r > s \) is tangent to both axes and to the second and third circles. What is \( r/s \)?

Then, the distance between the centers of the first and second circles should be equal to the sum of their radii (since they're tangent). But both have radius s, so the distance between centers should be 2s.

The first circle's center is at (s, s), and the second's is at (x2, s). The distance between them is |x2 - s| horizontally, since they're on the same horizontal line (same y-coordinate). So |x2 - s| = 2s. Since x2 must be greater than s (because it's in the first quadrant and to the right of the first circle), x2 - s = 2s => x2 = 3s. So the center of the second circle is at (3s, s). Wait, but if the center is at (3s, s) and the radius is s, then the circle would extend from x = 3s - s = 2s to x = 3s + s = 4s, and from y = 0 to y = 2s. But this seems okay as it's tangent to the x-axis (y=0) and the first circle is at (s, s). So the distance between centers is 2s, which is correct since 3s - s = 2s.

Similarly, the third circle is tangent to the first circle and the y-axis. So its center must be at (s, y3), but since it's tangent to the y-axis, the x-coordinate is s. The y-coordinate would then be... wait, same logic. The center is (s, y3). The radius is s, so to be tangent to the y-axis, x-coordinate is s. Wait, no. Wait, the circle is tangent to the y-axis (x=0), so the distance from the center to the y-axis must be equal to the radius s. Therefore, the x-coordinate of the center is s. So the center is (s, y3). Now, the distance between this center and the first circle's center (s, s) must be 2s, since they are both radius s and tangent. So the vertical distance is |y3 - s| = 2s. Since it's above the first circle, y3 = s + 2s = 3s. So the third circle's center is (s, 3s). Hmm, that seems right. So second circle is at (3s, s), third at (s, 3s).

Now, the fourth circle, with radius r > s, is tangent to both axes, so its center is at (r, r). It's also tangent to the second and third circles. So the distance between the center of the fourth circle (r, r) and the second circle (3s, s) must be equal to r + s. Similarly, the distance between (r, r) and (s, 3s) must also be r + s.

Let's write those equations. First, distance between (r, r) and (3s, s):

√[(r - 3s)^2 + (r - s)^2] = r + s

Similarly, distance between (r, r) and (s, 3s):

√[(r - s)^2 + (r - 3s)^2] = r + s

Wait, that's the same equation because swapping the coordinates doesn't change the distance. So we just have one equation here. Let's square both sides to eliminate the square root:

(r - 3s)^2 + (r - s)^2 = (r + s)^2

Expanding the left-hand side:

First term: (r - 3s)^2 = r^2 - 6rs + 9s^2

Second term: (r - s)^2 = r^2 - 2rs + s^2

Adding them together: 2r^2 - 8rs + 10s^2

Right-hand side: (r + s)^2 = r^2 + 2rs + s^2

Set them equal:

2r^2 - 8rs + 10s^2 = r^2 + 2rs + s^2

Subtract the right-hand side from both sides:

2r^2 -8rs +10s^2 - r^2 -2rs -s^2 = 0

Simplify:

r^2 -10rs +9s^2 = 0

This is a quadratic in terms of r/s. Let's set k = r/s, so r = k s. Substitute into the equation:

(k s)^2 -10(k s)s +9s^2 = 0

Divide both sides by s^2 (since s ≠ 0):

k^2 -10k +9 = 0

Solving quadratic equation:

k = [10 ± √(100 - 36)]/2 = [10 ± √64]/2 = [10 ±8]/2

So two solutions: (10 +8)/2 = 18/2 =9, and (10-8)/2=2/2=1.

But since r > s, so k = r/s >1, so the solution is k=9. Therefore, r/s =9.

Wait, but let me check this. If r=9s, then the center is at (9s,9s). Let's compute the distance to the second circle at (3s, s):

Distance squared would be (9s -3s)^2 + (9s -s)^2 = (6s)^2 + (8s)^2 =36s² +64s²=100s², so distance is 10s. Then, since the sum of radii is r + s =9s +s=10s, which matches. So that works. Similarly, the distance to the third circle (s,3s) is the same.

But let me check if the circle at (9s,9s) is tangent to both axes. The distance to the x-axis is 9s, which is the radius, so yes. Similarly for the y-axis. So that seems correct.

        # display<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    __shared__ float smem[THREADS_PER_BLOCK];
    
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    const int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    
    const float* row_x = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* row_y = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    // Initialize for max reduction
    float thread_max = -1e20;
    
    // Vectorized load and max computation
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, __ldg(row_x + i));
    }
    
    // Warp-level reduction (no divergent branches)
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        float other = __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, offset);
        thread_max = max(thread_max, other);
    }
    
    // First thread in each warp writes to shared memory
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        smem[warp_id] = thread_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction in first warp
    if (tid < (THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE)) {
        thread_max = smem[tid];
    }
    
    if (tid < WARP_SIZE) {
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE)/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
            float other = __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, offset);
            thread_max = max(thread_max, other);
        }
    }
    
    // Broadcast max value to all threads
    float max_val = __shfl_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, 0);
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Compute exp and sum
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        float val = __expf(__ldg(row_x + i) - max_val);
        row_y[i] = val;  // Store intermediate result
        thread_sum += val;
    }
    
    // Warp-level sum reduction
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        thread_sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, offset);
    }
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        smem[warp_id] = thread_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    if (tid < (THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE)) {
        thread_sum = smem[tid];
    }
    
    if (tid < WARP_SIZE) {
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE)/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
            thread_sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, offset);
        }
    }
    
    float sum_val = __shfl_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, 0);
    
    // Normalize with vectorized operations
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        row_y[i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size);
    dim3 block(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, block>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

</think>To solve the problem, we need to determine the ratio \( \frac{r}{s} \) where \( r \) is the radius of a larger circle that is tangent to both axes and to two smaller circles of radius \( s \). Here are the detailed steps:

1. **Position of the first circle:**
   - The first circle is tangent to both the \( x \)-axis and \( y \)-axis.
   - Therefore, its center is at \( (s, s) \).

2. **Position of the second circle:**
   - The second circle is tangent to the first circle and the \( x \)-axis.
   - Since it is tangent to the \( x \)-axis, its center is at \( (x_2, s) \).
   - The distance between the centers of the first and second circles is \( 2s \) (since both have radius \( s \)).
   - Hence, \( x_2 - s = 2s \) which gives \( x_2 = 3s \).
   - So, the center of the second circle is at \( (3s, s) \).

3. **Position of the third circle:**
   - The third circle is tangent to the first circle and the \( y \)-axis.
   - Since it is tangent to the \( y \)-axis, its center is at \( (s, y_3) \).
   - The distance between the centers of the first and third circles is \( 2s \).
   - Hence, \( y_3 - s = 2s \) which gives \( y_3 = 3s \).
   - So, the center of the third circle is at \( (s, 3s) \).

4. **Position of the larger circle:**
   - The larger circle is tangent to both axes, so its center is at \( (r, r) \).
   - It is also tangent to the second and third circles.
   - The distance from the center of the larger circle \( (r, r) \) to the center of the second circle \( (3s, s) \) is \( r + s \).
   - The distance formula gives:
     \[
     \sqrt{(r - 3s)^2 + (r - s)^2} = r + s
     \]
   - Squaring both sides:
     \[
     (r - 3s)^2 + (r - s)^2 = (r + s)^2
     \]
   - Expanding both sides:
     \[
     (r^2 - 6rs + 9s^2) + (r^2 - 2rs + s^2) = r^2 + 2rs + s^2
     \]
     \[
     2r^2 - 8rs + 10s^2 = r^2 + 2rs + s^2
     \]
   - Simplifying:
     \[
     r^2 - 10rs + 9s^2 = 0
     \]
   - This is a quadratic equation in terms of \( \frac{r}{s} \). Let \( k = \frac{r}{s} \), so \( r = ks \).
   - Substituting \( r = ks \) into the equation:
     \[
     (ks)^2 - 10(ks)s + 9s^2 = 0
     \]
     \[
     k^2s^2 - 10ks^2 + 9s^2 = 0
     \]
     \[
     k^2 - 10k + 9 = 0
     \]
   - Solving the quadratic equation:
     \[
     k = \frac{10 \pm \sqrt{100 - 36}}{2} = \frac{10 \pm \sqrt{64}}{2} = \frac{10 \pm 8}{2}
     \]
     \[
     k = 9 \quad \text{or} \quad k = 1
     \]
   - Since \( r > s \), we discard \( k = 1 \) and accept \( k = 9 \).

Therefore, the ratio \( \frac{r}{s} \) is:
\[
\boxed{9}
\]<#sep#>.

=============================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================== 2 failed, 2 passed, 26 warnings in 0.<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

__device__ __forceinline__ float warp_reduce_max(float val) {
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2)
        val = max(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset));
    return val;
}

__device__ __forceinline__ float warp_reduce_sum(float val) {
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2)
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset);
    return val;
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, 
                             float* __restrict__ y, 
                             const int num_features) {
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    const int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    const int warps_per_block = THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE;
    
    // Calculate optimal stride based on feature size
    const int stride = THREADS_PER_BLOCK;
    
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    __shared__ float warp_maxes[warps_per_block];
    __shared__ float warp_sums[warps_per_block];

    // Find max value
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        if (i < num_features) {
            thread_max = max(thread_max, __ldg(&x_row[i]));
        }
    }
    
    // Warp-level reduction
    float warp_max = warp_reduce_max(thread_max);
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_maxes[warp_id] = warp_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction for max
    if (tid < warps_per_block) {
        float block_max = warp_maxes[tid];
        block_max = warp_reduce_max(block_max);
        if (tid == 0) {
            warp_maxes[0] = block_max;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    const float max_val = warp_maxes[0];
    
    // Compute exp and sum
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        if (i < num_features) {
            const float val = __expf(__ldg(&x_row[i]) - max_val);
            y_row[i] = val;
            thread_sum += val;
        }
    }
    
    // Warp-level reduction for sum
    float warp_sum = warp_reduce_sum(thread_sum);
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_sums[warp_id] = warp_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction for sum
    if (tid < warps_per_block) {
        float block_sum = warp_sums[tid];
        block_sum = warp_reduce_sum(block_sum);
        if (tid == 0) {
            warp_sums[0] = block_sum;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    const float sum_val = warp_sums[0];
    
    // Normalize with optimized stride
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        if (i < num_features) {
            y_row[i] /= sum_val;
        }
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size);
    dim3 block(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, block>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}<#sep#>Given \( A \in M_n(\mathbb{C}) \) with \( \text{Rank}(A) = r \), find the number of linearly independent solutions to the equation \( AX + XA = O \), where \( X \in M_n(\mathbb{C}) \).

<think>
Okay, so I have this problem here where I need to find the number of linearly independent solutions to the matrix equation AX + XA = O, where A is an n×n complex matrix with rank r. Hmm, let me think. 

First, I remember that when dealing with matrix equations like this, especially ones that are linear in X, it might be helpful to vectorize the matrices. Vectorization turns a matrix into a vector by stacking its columns. So, if I vec(X) is the vectorized form of X, then the equation AX + XA = O can be rewritten using Kronecker products. The formula for that is (I ⊗ A + A^T ⊗ I) vec(X) = 0. Is that right? Let me verify. 

Yes, because AX corresponds to (I ⊗ A) vec(X) and XA corresponds to (A^T ⊗ I) vec(X). So combining these gives the Kronecker sum. Therefore, the solution space is the null space of the matrix (I ⊗ A + A^T ⊗ I). So the number of linearly independent solutions would be the nullity of this matrix. Since the nullity is the dimension of the null space, which is equal to the total number of columns minus the rank of the matrix. But wait, the matrix here is n² × n², so the nullity would be n² - rank(I ⊗ A + A^T ⊗ I). 

But how do I find the rank of (I ⊗ A + A^T ⊗ I)? That seems tricky. Maybe I need to diagonalize A or use its Jordan canonical form since we're working over the complex numbers. Since A is a complex matrix, it can be put into Jordan canonical form. Let's suppose that A is similar to its Jordan form J, so A = PJP^{-1} for some invertible P. Then, the equation AX + XA = O becomes PJP^{-1}X + XPJP^{-1} = O. Multiplying both sides on the left by P^{-1} and on the right by P, we get J(P^{-1}XP) + (P^{-1}XP)J = O. Let me denote Y = P^{-1}XP. Then the equation becomes JY + YJ = O. So, the original equation is similar to JY + YJ = O, which would have the same number of solutions as the original equation since similarity transformations are invertible. Therefore, the number of solutions is invariant under similarity, so we can assume without loss of generality that A is in Jordan canonical form.

Alternatively, perhaps even upper triangular form, but Jordan form might be more helpful. However, even so, dealing with the Jordan form might complicate things. Maybe there's a better approach.

Alternatively, consider eigenvalues. If A is diagonalizable, which it is over the complex numbers if it's diagonalizable (but not all matrices are diagonalizable, but since we can use Jordan form, maybe we can consider the diagonal case first). Suppose A is diagonal. Then, in that case, A = diag(λ₁, λ₂, ..., λ_n). Then, the equation AX + XA = O would imply that for each entry x_{ij} of X, we have (λ_i + λ_j)x_{ij} = 0. So, the solution X would have entries x_{ij} = 0 whenever λ_i + λ_j ≠ 0, and x_{ij} can be arbitrary when λ_i + λ_j = 0. Therefore, the number of free variables would be the number of pairs (i,j) such that λ_i + λ_j = 0. 

But A has rank r, so how does that affect the eigenvalues? Wait, if A is diagonal with rank r, then it has r non-zero eigenvalues and n - r zero eigenvalues. However, the eigenvalues can be paired such that λ_i + λ_j = 0. So, if there are k eigenvalues that are non-zero and come in pairs (λ, -λ), then each such pair contributes some solutions. But maybe this is getting too specific. Alternatively, in the diagonalizable case, the number of solutions would be the sum over all i,j where λ_i + λ_j = 0. 

But since the problem states that A is any complex matrix with rank r, we might need a formula that depends only on n and r. So perhaps there's a general formula here. Wait, when A is invertible (r = n), then the equation AX + XA = 0. If A is invertible, then I think the only solution is X = 0. Because if A is invertible, then the eigenvalues are non-zero, and if all λ_i + λ_j ≠ 0, which would require that if λ is an eigenvalue, then -λ is not. But that's not necessarily true. For example, if A is the identity matrix, then AX + XA = 0 becomes X + X = 0, so X = 0. But if A is diagonal with entries 1 and -1, then there might be solutions. Wait, but in the problem statement, A is of rank r, so if A is invertible (r = n), then in the case where A has eigenvalues with their negatives also present, then there could be solutions. But maybe the answer depends on the structure of A's eigenvalues. But the problem states "Given A ∈ M_n(ℂ) with Rank(A) = r", so the answer should depend only on n and r. Hmm. But how? Maybe there's a result from matrix equations.

Wait, I recall that the dimension of the solution space of AX + XA^T = 0 is something like (n + rank(A))/2 choose 2 or something, but I might be confusing it with another equation. Alternatively, perhaps Sylvester equation. The equation AX + XA = 0 is a special case of the Sylvester equation AX + XB = C, where here B = A and C = O. The Sylvester equation has a unique solution when A and -B have disjoint spectra. In our case, B = A, so we require that A and -A have disjoint spectra. But since they are the same except for sign, their spectra are {λ_i} and {-λ_i}, so they are disjoint only if no eigenvalue is the negative of another. But in our case, since we're over complex numbers, and A can have eigenvalues in any places, so depending on the eigenvalues, the solution space can vary. #include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// Constant memory for storing frequently accessed data
__constant__ float const_data[THREADS_PER_BLOCK];

// CUDA kernel declaration
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features);

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);

    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Load data into constant memory
    if (tid < num_features) {
        const_data[tid] = x_row[tid];
    }
    __syncthreads();

    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = const_data[i];
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }

    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s && (tid + s) < stride) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = const_data[i];
        float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s && (tid + s) < stride) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}<#sep#> 

Alternatively, perhaps consider eigenvalues. If A has eigenvalues λ₁, ..., λ_n, then the eigenvalues of the operator L are λ_i + λ_j for all i, j. Thus, the dimension of the kernel is the number of pairs (i,j) such that λ_i + λ_j = 0. 

So, the number of solutions would be equal to the number of pairs (i,j) where λ_i = -λ_j. But how does this relate to the rank of A? 

Since the rank of A is r, the number of non-zero eigenvalues (counting algebraic multiplicities) is r. However, eigenvalues can come in pairs. For example, if λ is an eigenvalue, so is -λ. But not necessarily. So, if A has rank r, it has r non-zero eigenvalues and n - r zero eigenvalues. assistant: Let me check the imports<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// CUDA kernel declaration
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features);

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);

    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Load data into shared memory
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = x_row[i];
        sdata[tid] = val;
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce max value
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Compute exponentials and sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(sdata[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce sum
    sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Normalize
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

Now, using properties of Kronecker products. The rank of a Kronecker product is the product of the ranks. However, here we have a sum of two Kronecker products. There is no straightforward formula for the rank of a sum of Kronecker products, but maybe we can relate it to the eigenvalues. 

Alternatively, if A is diagonalizable, let me consider that case. Suppose A is diagonalizable, so A = PDP^{-1}, where D is diagonal. Then, the operator L(X) = AX + XA is similar to the operator L'(Y) = DY + YD, where Y = P^{-1}XP. Therefore, the solution spaces are isomorphic, so the nullity is the same. If D is diagonal with entries λ₁, ..., λ_n, then as before, the nullity is the number of pairs (i,j) with λ_i + λ_j = 0. 

But how to express this in terms of rank? Suppose the rank of A is r, so there are r non-zero eigenvalues (counting multiplicities) and n - r zeros. Let me denote m = n - r. Then, the number of pairs (i,j) where λ_i + λ_j = 0 would be equal to the number of pairs (i,j) where both λ_i and λ_j are zero, which is m², plus the number of pairs (i,j) where λ_i = -λ_j and both are non-zero. Let's call the number of such pairs as t. 

But how can we compute t? The problem is that t depends on the specific eigenvalues of A, which are not given. However, the problem asks for the number of solutions in terms of n and r. Therefore, perhaps t must be expressed in terms of r. But unless there's a relation that ties t to r, which is not obvious. 

Wait a minute, maybe over the complex field, the number of pairs (i,j) with λ_i + λ_j = 0 can be related to the Jordan blocks. Alternatively, perhaps the answer is m² + something. But in the absence of specific information about the eigenvalues, maybe the minimal or maximal possible nullity is considered? But the problem states "find the number of linearly independent solutions", implying that it's uniquely determined by n and r, so there must be a standard formula.

Wait, perhaps another approach. Suppose that X is a solution to AX + XA = 0. Then, multiplying both sides by A on the left, we get A²X + AXA = 0. Similarly, multiplying on the right by A gives AXA + XA² = 0. Subtracting these two equations gives A²X - XA² = 0. So, X commutes with A². Hmm, but not sure if this helps. 

Alternatively, if A is rank r, then the image of A is r-dimensional. So, for X in the solution space, the image of X must lie in the kernel of A? Wait, not necessarily. Let me think. If AX + XA = 0, then AX = -XA. So, if v is a vector, then AXv = -XAv. If v is in the kernel of A, then Av = 0, so AXv = -X0 = 0, so Xv must be in the kernel of A. Therefore, X maps ker(A) to ker(A). Similarly, if v is in the image of A, then XAv = -AXv, but Av is in the image of A. So, if X maps im(A) to im(A), then AXv is in A(im(A)) which is im(A²). However, since A has rank r, im(A²) might have lower rank. This seems a bit too vague. 

Alternatively, note that ker(A) has dimension n - r. Let me consider X as a linear operator. Then, for X to satisfy AX = -XA, perhaps X must intertwine A and -A. That is, X is a linear transformation such that XA = -AX. Therefore, X is a module homomorphism between the modules defined by A and -A. The dimension of such homomorphisms is given by the dimension of Hom(V_A, V_{-A}), where V_A is the vector space seen as an A-module. The dimension of this space is equal to the number of Jordan blocks shared between A and -A, multiplied by some multiplicities. But this seems too abstract. 

Alternatively, using the formula from the solution of the Sylvester equation. The equation AX + XB = C has a unique solution if and only if A and -B have no eigenvalues in common. In our case, B = A, so we require that A and -A have no eigenvalues in common. But in the complex field, eigenvalues can be anything. However, if A has eigenvalues λ, then -A has eigenvalues -λ. So, if A has no eigenvalues that are negatives of each other, then the only solution is X = 0. But if there are eigenvalues λ and -λ, then there might be non-trivial solutions. However, since we are not given specific eigenvalues, but only the rank, this line of thought might not be helpful. 

Wait, but maybe there's a relation between the rank of A and the number of solutions. Suppose that the solution space is isomorphic to the space of matrices that commute with A when multiplied by -1. But I don't know. 

Alternatively, let me think of specific cases to see a pattern.

Case 1: Let A be the zero matrix. Then Rank(A) = 0. The equation becomes 0X + X0 = 0, which is always true. So, all matrices X are solutions. Therefore, the number of solutions is n². So, in this case, the answer is n².

Case 2: Let A be invertible (r = n). Then, if A is invertible, does the equation AX + XA = 0 have only the trivial solution? For example, take A = I. Then, the equation is IX + XI = 2X = 0, so X = 0. So, only the trivial solution. But suppose A is diagonal with entries (1, -1, 0, ..., 0). Wait, but if A is invertible, then all eigenvalues are non-zero. If A is diagonal with entries 1 and -1, then the equation AX + XA = 0 would allow for X having non-zero entries where the corresponding eigenvalues add to zero. For example, if A is diag(1, -1), then in the 2x2 case, the solution X would have x_{12} and x_{21} as free variables because 1 + (-1) = 0. So, the solutions are matrices of the form [[0, a], [b, 0]], which has 2 dimensions. So, in this case, even when A is invertible (rank 2), there are non-trivial solutions. Therefore, the number of solutions isn't necessarily zero when A is invertible. Therefore, the previous assumption was wrong. Hence, the answer depends on more than just the rank. But the problem states that the rank is given, so there must be a way to express the answer in terms of the rank and n. 

Wait, but in the case when A is invertible (r = n), but has eigenvalues λ and -λ, the number of solutions would be 2 if A is 2x2 with eigenvalues 1 and -1. So, the answer would be 2. But in that case, n = 2 and r = 2, so it's not simply dependent on n and r. Therefore, the previous approaches must be missing something.

That suggests that either the answer is the same regardless of A (as long as rank r) or there is a formula in terms of n and r. However, in the examples I considered, when A is invertible, the number of solutions can vary. For example, if A is the identity matrix (rank n), then AX + XA = 0 implies X = 0. ## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS

### 5.1 Problem Statement
The `sepal_ui.Btn` class has two issues:
1. **Default text problem**: Buttons automatically show "Click" text when no `msg` is provided
2. **Icon alignment problem**: Icons are always left-aligned (`left=True`) even when there's no text, causing misalignment

### 5.2 Problem Location
File: `/testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/btn.py`
- Line 39: Default `msg="Click"` should be `msg=""`
- Line 58: Icon always created with `left=True` 
- Lines 86-94: `_set_text` method doesn't adjust icon alignment

### 5.3 Test Reproduction
The reproduction script confirms:
- Default button shows "Click" text instead of empty
- Icon-only buttons have `left=True` instead of `left=False`

### 5.4 Best Practices
- Maintain backward compatibility where possible
- Make minimal changes to existing code
- Follow existing code patterns in the file
- Ensure icon alignment logic is consistent with test expectations

### 5.5 Fix Strategy
1. Change default `msg` parameter from "Click" to ""
2. Add logic in `_set_text` method to dynamically set icon `left` property:
   - If text is empty: set `left=False` (centered)
   - If text has content: set `left=True` (left-aligned)

#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

template<bool IsAligned>
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    // Ensure 128-bit alignment
    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * ((num_features + 3) & ~3);
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * ((num_features + 3) & ~3);

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    if (IsAligned) {
        // Process 4 elements at a time when aligned
        for (int i = tid * 4; i < num_features; i += stride * 4) {
            float4 val4;
            if (i + 3 < num_features) {
                val4 = *reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(__ldg(x_row + i));
                max_val = max(max_val, max(max(val4.x, val4.y), max(val4.z, val4.w)));
            } else {
                for (int j = 0; j < 4 && i + j < num_features; j++) {
                    float val = __ldg(&x_row[i + j]);
                    max_val = max(max_val, val);
                }
            }
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
            float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
            max_val = max(max_val, val);
        }
    }

    sdata[tid] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    if (IsAligned) {
        for (int i = tid * 4; i < num_features; i += stride * 4) {
            float4 out4;
            if (i + 3 < num_features) {
                float4 val4 = *reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(__ldg(x_row + i));
                out4.x = __expf(val4.x - max_val);
                out4.y = __expf(val4.y - max_val);
                out4.z = __expf(val4.z - max_val);
                out4.w = __expf(val4.w - max_val);
                *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = out4;
                sum_val += out4.x + out4.y + out4.z + out4.w;
            } else {
                for (int j = 0; j < 4 && i + j < num_features; j++) {
                    float val = __ldg(&x_row[i + j]);
                    float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
                    y_row[i + j] = exp_val;
                    sum_val += exp_val;
                }
            }
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
            float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
            float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
            y_row[i] = exp_val;
            sum_val += exp_val;
        }
    }

    sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    if (IsAligned) {
        for (int i = tid * 4; i < num_features; i += stride * 4) {
            if (i + 3 < num_features) {
                float4 val4 = *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i);
                val4.x /= sum_val;
                val4.y /= sum_val;
                val4.z /= sum_val;
                val4.w /= sum_val;
                *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = val4;
            } else {
                for (int j = 0; j < 4 && i + j < num_features; j++) {
                    y_row[i + j] /= sum_val;
                }
            }
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
            y_row[i] /= sum_val;
        }
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;
    
    if (num_features % 4 == 0) {
        softmax_kernel<true><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
    } else {
        softmax_kernel<false><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

He loves it so much that he rides it through rain, snow, wind and ice. Some day, he will be able to fly with it. This weekend, Emil is on a vacation in New York. He has compiled a list of cool places to visit, as well as a list of all one-way streets between these places, and now it is time for some sightseeing! 

One sightseeing trip consists of $S$ places and exactly $S$ streets which connect them. He starts by travelling to one of these places by subway, with his moped on his back. Then he starts riding his moped to each place of the trip in turn, finally returning to where he started in the current trip. Then he goes back to the subway, with his moped on his back, and goes for the next trip. He does this until he has enjoyed all of the trips.

Since it’s very hard to determine if Emil can visit all of the places he wants to see in a single trip of this kind, he has instead asked you to help him split all of the $N$ places in town into a number of trips, such that each place in town is included in exactly one trip.

Given all the places Emil wants to see and the streets of the city connecting these, construct a list of trips fulfilling these conditions, or determine that it is impossible. Each trip must use at least one street, and there may be loops, i.e. streets with a start and end point in the same place.

-----Input-----
The first line of input consists of two integers $1\le N \le 100$ and $0 \le M \le 10000$, the number of places Emil wants to visit and the number of roads respectively. The places are numbered $0$ to $N-1$.

The next $M$ lines consists of two integers $0 \le f, t \le N-1$, denoting that there is a one-way street from place $f$ to place $t$.

-----Output-----
Output begins with an integer $N$ on its own line, the number of trips made. Then, for every trip, output a line with the number of places included in the trip $K$, followed by $K$ lines, containing the numbers of said places in order of visit (the last place which Emil returns to should not be output twice).

If the places can’t be split into trips, Emil instead goes bowling. Then just output the string "Yeah!". If there are several solutions, output any of them.

-----Examples-----
Sample Input 1:
4 4
0 1
1 0
2 3
3 2
Sample Output 1:
2
2
0
1
2
2
3

Sample Input 2:
4 4
0 1
1 0
2 3
3 3
Sample Output 2:

1. Default button (no parameters):

   Icon visible: True<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define MAX_BLOCKS 256

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, 
                             float* __restrict__ y,
                             const int num_features,
                             const int batch_size,
                             const int rows_per_block) {
    extern __shared__ float smem[];
    float* sdata = smem;
    
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int lane = tid & 31;
    const int wid = tid >> 5;
    
    // Calculate which rows this block handles
    const int row_start = blockIdx.x * rows_per_block;
    const int row_end = min(row_start + rows_per_block, batch_size);
    
    // Process multiple rows if needed
    for (int row = row_start; row < row_end; row++) {
        const float* x_row = x + row * num_features;
        float* y_row = y + row * num_features;
        
        // Find max value
        float thread_max = -INFINITY;
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
            thread_max = max(thread_max, __ldg(&x_row[i]));
        }
        
        sdata[tid] = thread_max;
        __syncthreads();
        
        // Reduce max within block
        if (tid < 128) sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + 128]);
        __syncthreads();
        if (tid < 64) sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + 64]);
        __syncthreads();
        if (tid < 32) {
            volatile float* vmem = sdata;
            vmem[tid] = max(vmem[tid], vmem[tid + 32]);
            vmem[tid] = max(vmem[tid], vmem[tid + 16]);
            vmem[tid] = max(vmem[tid], vmem[tid + 8]);
            vmem[tid] = max(vmem[tid], vmem[tid + 4]);
            vmem[tid] = max(vmem[tid], vmem[tid + 2]);
            vmem[tid] = max(vmem[tid], vmem[tid + 1]);
        }
        
        const float max_val = sdata[0];
        __syncthreads();
        
        // Compute exp and sum
        float thread_sum = 0.0f;
        const int stride = THREADS_PER_BLOCK;
        #pragma unroll 4
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
            const float val = __expf(__ldg(&x_row[i]) - max_val);
            y_row[i] = val;
            thread_sum += val;
        }
        
        // Sum reduction
        sdata[tid] = thread_sum;
        __syncthreads();
        
        if (tid < 128) sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + 128];
        __syncthreads();
        if (tid < 64) sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + 64];
        __syncthreads();
        if (tid < 32) {
            volatile float* vmem = sdata;
            vmem[tid] += vmem[tid + 32];
            vmem[tid] += vmem[tid + 16];
            vmem[tid] += vmem[tid + 8];
            vmem[tid] += vmem[tid + 4];
            vmem[tid] += vmem[tid + 2];
            vmem[tid] += vmem[tid + 1];
        }
        
        const float sum_val = sdata[0];
        __syncthreads();
        
        // Normalize with stride pattern
        #pragma unroll 4
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
            y_row[i] /= sum_val;
        }
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    const int rows_per_block = max(1, batch_size / MAX_BLOCKS + (batch_size % MAX_BLOCKS != 0));
    const int grid_dim = min(MAX_BLOCKS, (batch_size + rows_per_block - 1) / rows_per_block);
    
    dim3 grid(grid_dim);
    dim3 block(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    
    const int smem_size = THREADS_PER_BLOCK * sizeof(float);
    
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, block, smem_size>>>(x, y, num_features, batch_size, rows_per_block);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// Constant memory for storing frequently accessed data
__constant__ float const_data[THREADS_PER_BLOCK];

// CUDA kernel declaration
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features);

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);

    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Load data into constant memory
    if (tid < num_features) {
        const_data[tid] = (tid < num_features) ? x_row[tid] : 0.0f;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = const_data[i];
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }

    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s && (tid + s) < stride) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = const_data[i];
        float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s && (tid + s) < stride) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/inputs.py:974
  /testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/inputs.py:974: DeprecationWarning: Call to deprecated function (or staticmethod) switch. (<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// CUDA kernel declaration
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features);

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);

    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Load data and find max value
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = x_row[i];
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }
    
    // Store max_val in shared memory for reduction
    sdata[tid] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce max value
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Compute exponentials and sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(sdata[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce sum
    sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Normalize
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

Another approach. Suppose that the solution space is isomorphic to the space of matrices X such that X maps ker A to ker A and im A to im A, and X interchanges these spaces appropriately. Let me consider the decomposition of the vector space into ker A and a complement. But since A maps the complement to im A. 

Let me write X in block form according to the decomposition ker A and im A. Let's say A has rank r, so dim ker A = n - r. Let’s choose a basis such that the first n - r vectors are a basis for ker A and the remaining r vectors are a basis for im A. Then, in this basis, matrix A can be written as:

A = [0   B
     0   C]

But since A maps im A to itself (because A(im A) is a subset of im A), then B = 0 and C is an r×r matrix of rank r (since overall rank of A is r). So, in this basis, A is:

A = [0   0
     0   C]

where C is invertible. 

Now, let's write X in block form:

X = [X₁₁ X₁₂
     X₂₁ X₂₂]

where X₁₁ is (n - r)×(n - r), X₁₂ is (n - r)×r, X₂₁ is r×(n - r), and X₂₂ is r×r.

Then, the equation AX + XA = 0 becomes:

[0   0         [X₁₁ X₁₂      [X₁₁ X₁₂     [0   0
 0   C]        X₂₁ X₂₂]  +   X₂₁ X₂₂]    0   C] = 0

Calculating the first term:

AX = [0*X₁₁ + 0*X₂₁   0*X₁₂ + 0*X₂₂
       C*X₂₁           C*X₂₂]

And XA:

XA = [X₁₁*0 + X₁₂*0   X₁₁*0 + X₁₂*C
       X₂₁*0 + X₂₂*0   X₂₁*0 + X₂₂*C]

Simplifying:

AX = [0           0
      C X₂₁     C X₂₂]

XA = [0          X₁₂ C
      0          X₂₂ C]

Adding AX + XA:

[0 + 0           0 + X₁₂ C
 C X₂₁ + 0     C X₂₂ + X₂₂ C]

Setting this equal to the zero matrix, we get the following equations:

1. C X₂₁ = 0
2. X₁₂ C = 0
3. C X₂₂ + X₂₂ C = 0

Since C is invertible (because it's r×r and rank r), from equation 1: C X₂₁ = 0 implies X₂₁ = 0. Similarly, equation 2: X₁₂ C = 0 implies X₁₂ = 0. Equation 3: C X₂₂ + X₂₂ C = 0.

Therefore, the only non-zero blocks are X₁₁ and X₂₂, with X₂₂ satisfying C X₂₂ + X₂₂ C = 0. 

Now, X₁₁ is arbitrary (n - r)×(n - r) matrix. The dimension of X₁₁ is (n - r)². 

For X₂₂, we have the equation C X₂₂ + X₂₂ C = 0. Let's denote D = X₂₂. The equation becomes C D + D C = 0. Since C is invertible, this is equivalent to D = -C^{-1} D C. 

This is a Sylvester equation, and since C and -C are both invertible and their spectra are negatives of each other, the only solution is D = 0 if C and -C have no common eigenvalues. But C is an invertible r×r matrix. If C has eigenvalues λ_i, then -C has eigenvalues -λ_i. So, if C has no eigenvalues that are negatives of each other, then the only solution is D = 0. But if C has eigenvalues that are negatives of each other, there can be non-zero solutions. 

But here's the problem: depending on the matrix C (which is arbitrary invertible r×r matrix), the solution space for D can vary. For example, if C is diagonal with distinct eigenvalues, then D must be zero. If C is the identity matrix, then the equation becomes D + D = 0, so D = 0. If C is diagonal with entries (1, -1), then D must satisfy D = -C^{-1} D C. Let's compute this for a 2x2 case:

C = diag(1, -1). Then, C^{-1} = C. Then, D = -C D C. Let D = [[a, b], [c, d]]. Then:

C D C = [[1*1*a, 1*(-1)*b], [(-1)*1*c, (-1)*(-1)*d]] = [[a, -b], [-c, d]]

Thus, -C D C = [[-a, b], [c, -d]]. Setting this equal to D:

[[a, b], [c, d]] = [[-a, b], [c, -d]]

This implies a = -a, d = -d, so a = d = 0. However, b and c can be arbitrary. Therefore, D = [[0, b], [c, 0]], which is 2-dimensional. 

So, in this case, D has dimension 2. Hence, the solution space for X₂₂ is 2-dimensional. 

But since the problem states that A is given with rank r, but nothing about its specific structure, the solution space for D can vary. def find_cycle(graph):
    visited = set()
    path = []
    start_node = pick a starting node (with outgoing edges)
    def dfs(node):
        path.append(node)
        visited.add(node)
        for neighbor in graph[node]:
            if neighbor not in visited:
                if dfs(neighbor):
                    return True
                else:
                    path.pop()
            else:
                if neighbor is in path:
                    # Found a cycle: from the index of neighbor in path +1 to include the current node?
                    # Because path is the path from start to current node, and neighbor is already in path.
                    index = path.index(neighbor)
                    cycle = path[index:] + [neighbor]
                    return True
                else:
                    pass # already visited in a different branch, but no cycle
        return False
    # Then call this function.

   5.2<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

template<bool IsAligned>
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    // Ensure 128-bit alignment
    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * ((num_features + 3) & ~3);
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * ((num_features + 3) & ~3);

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    if (IsAligned) {
        // Process 4 elements at a time when aligned
        for (int i = tid * 4; i < num_features; i += stride * 4) {
            float4 val4;
            if (i + 3 < num_features) {
                val4 = *reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i);
                max_val = max(max_val, max(max(val4.x, val4.y), max(val4.z, val4.w)));
            } else {
                for (int j = 0; j < 4 && i + j < num_features; j++) {
                    float val = __ldg(&x_row[i + j]);
                    max_val = max(max_val, val);
                }
            }
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
            float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
            max_val = max(max_val, val);
        }
    }

    sdata[tid] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    if (IsAligned) {
        for (int i = tid * 4; i < num_features; i += stride * 4) {
            float4 out4;
            if (i + 3 < num_features) {
                float4 val4 = *reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i);
                out4.x = __expf(val4.x - max_val);
                out4.y = __expf(val4.y - max_val);
                out4.z = __expf(val4.z - max_val);
                out4.w = __expf(val4.w - max_val);
                *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = out4;
                sum_val += out4.x + out4.y + out4.z + out4.w;
            } else {
                for (int j = 0; j < 4 && i + j < num_features; j++) {
                    float val = __ldg(&x_row[i + j]);
                    float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
                    y_row[i + j] = exp_val;
                    sum_val += exp_val;
                }
            }
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
            float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
            float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
            y_row[i] = exp_val;
            sum_val += exp_val;
        }
    }

    sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    if (IsAligned) {
        for (int i = tid * 4; i < num_features; i += stride * 4) {
            if (i + 3 < num_features) {
                float4 val4 = *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i);
                val4.x /= sum_val;
                val4.y /= sum_val;
                val4.z /= sum_val;
                val4.w /= sum_val;
                *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = val4;
            } else {
                for (int j = 0; j < 4 && i + j < num_features; j++) {
                    y_row[i + j] /= sum_val;
                }
            }
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
            y_row[i] /= sum_val;
        }
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;
    
    if (num_features % 4 == 0) {
        softmax_kernel<true><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
    } else {
        softmax_kernel<false><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem



tool: Your thought has been logged.

assistant: Let me reword the problem in clearer terms:

**Problem Summary:**
The `sepal_ui.Btn` component has an issue with icon-only buttons. When creating a button with `icon=True` and a glyph icon, the component automatically adds default text "click" if no message is provided. When users try to remove this text by setting empty strings, the icon becomes left-aligned instead of properly centered.

**Key Technical Details:**
- Component: `sepal_ui.Btn` (imported as `sw.Btn`)
- Parameters involved: `icon`, `gliph`, `msg`, `children`
- Default behavior: Adds "click" text when `msg` is not provided
- Issue: Empty text causes misalignment of icon

**Steps to Reproduce:**
1. Create `sw.Btn(icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")` - displays unwanted "click" text
2. Try `sw.Btn(children=[""], icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")` - icon left-aligned
3. Try `sw.Btn(msg="", icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")` - icon left-aligned

**Expected Behavior:**
- Icon-only buttons should display only the icon, centered properly
- No default text should be added when `icon=True` and no explicit message is provided
