But how do we compute |aQR + bQR| for non-zero a and b? This seems challenging. Maybe we can find a pattern or use some properties. Since multiplication by a non-zero scalar is a bijection, we can factor that out.

Note that if a and b are both non-zero, then S(a, b) = aQR + bQR = a(QR + (b/a)QR). Let’s denote d = b/a mod 11. Then S(a,b) = a(QR + d QR). Since a is invertible, multiplying by a just permutes the elements, so |S(a,b)| = |QR + d QR|. Therefore, the size of the sumset depends only on the ratio d = b/a. So instead of considering all pairs (a,b), we can consider all possible ratios d (i.e., d ranges over all non-zero elements of Z/11Z, which are 1 to 10). For each d, the number of pairs (a,b) with b/a ≡ d mod 11 is 10, since for each non-zero a, b is determined as b = d*a mod 11, and a can be any of 1 to 10. Therefore, each d corresponds to 10 pairs (a,b). Therefore, if for each d we compute |QR + d QR|, then multiply by 10, and sum over all d from 1 to 10, then add the 121 from the cases where a or b is zero, we can get the total number of triples.

Therefore, the key is to compute |QR + d QR| for each d in 1 to 10, then multiply each by 10, sum them, and add 121. So now, we need to find |QR + d QR| for each d.

    86	   <#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

template <int VEC_SIZE, bool ALIGNED>
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int vec_stride = blockDim.x * VEC_SIZE;
    __shared__ float sdata[(THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31) / 32];

    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Max reduction with vectorized LDG
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid * VEC_SIZE; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i));
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, fmaxf(fmaxf(vec.x, vec.y), fmaxf(vec.z, vec.w)));
        }
    } else {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __ldg(x_row + i));
        }
    }

    // Warp reduction for max
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
        max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));

    if (tid % 32 == 0)
        sdata[tid / 32] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    if (tid < 32) {
        max_val = tid < (blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : -INFINITY;
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));
        if (tid == 0)
            sdata[0] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    max_val = sdata[0];

    // Sum reduction with vectorized stores
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid * VEC_SIZE; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i));
            float4 exp_vec = {__expf(vec.x - max_val), __expf(vec.y - max_val),
                             __expf(vec.z - max_val), __expf(vec.w - max_val)};
            *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = exp_vec;
            sum_val += exp_vec.x + exp_vec.y + exp_vec.z + exp_vec.w;
        }
    } else {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
            float val = __ldg(x_row + i);
            float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
            y_row[i] = exp_val;
            sum_val += exp_val;
        }
    }

    // Warp reduction for sum
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
        sum_val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum_val, offset);

    if (tid % 32 == 0)
        sdata[tid / 32] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    if (tid < 32) {
        sum_val = tid < (blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : 0;
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
            sum_val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum_val, offset);
        if (tid == 0)
            sdata[0] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Normalization with vectorized stores
    const float inv_sum = 1.0f / sdata[0];
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid * VEC_SIZE; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i);
            vec.x *= inv_sum;
            vec.y *= inv_sum;
            vec.z *= inv_sum;
            vec.w *= inv_sum;
            *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = vec;
        }
    } else {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x)
            y_row[i] *= inv_sum;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    const int vec_size = 4;
    const bool aligned = (num_features % vec_size) == 0;
    const int shared_mem = ((THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31) / 32) * sizeof(float);

    if (aligned) {
        softmax_kernel<vec_size, true><<<batch_size, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
    } else {
        softmax_kernel<1, false><<<batch_size, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

  /test<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

template <int VEC_SIZE, bool ALIGNED>
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int vec_tid = tid * VEC_SIZE;
    const int vec_stride = blockDim.x * VEC_SIZE;

    __shared__ float sdata[(THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31) / 32];
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Vectorized max reduction
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll
        for (int i = vec_tid; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = *reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i);
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, fmaxf(fmaxf(vec.x, vec.y), fmaxf(vec.z, vec.w)));
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, x_row[i]);
        }
    }

    // Warp-level max reduction
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
        max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));

    if (tid % 32 == 0)
        sdata[tid / 32] = max_val;
    __syncthreads(); // Ensure maximum is written to shared memory

    // Final block max reduction
    if (tid < 32) {
        max_val = tid < (blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : -INFINITY;
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));
        if (tid == 0)
            sdata[0] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads(); // Ensure final max is available for all threads
    max_val = sdata[0];

    // Vectorized sum reduction
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll
        for (int i = vec_tid; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = *reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i);
            float4 exp_vec = {__expf(vec.x - max_val), __expf(vec.y - max_val),
                             __expf(vec.z - max_val), __expf(vec.w - max_val)};
            *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = exp_vec;
            sum_val += exp_vec.x + exp_vec.y + exp_vec.z + exp_vec.w;
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
            float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
            y_row[i] = exp_val;
            sum_val += exp_val;
        }
    }

    // Warp-level sum reduction
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
        sum_val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum_val, offset);

    if (tid % 32 == 0)
        sdata[tid / 32] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads(); // Ensure sums are written to shared memory

    // Final block sum reduction
    if (tid < 32) {
        sum_val = tid < (blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : 0;
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
            sum_val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum_val, offset);
        if (tid == 0)
            sdata[0] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads(); // Ensure normalized factor is available for all

    // Vectorized normalization
    const float inv_sum = 1.0f / sdata[0];
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll
        for (int i = vec_tid; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i);
            vec.x *= inv_sum;
            vec.y *= inv_sum;
            vec.z *= inv_sum;
            vec.w *= inv_sum;
            *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = vec;
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x)
            y_row[i] *= inv_sum;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    const int vec_size = 4;
    const bool aligned = (num_features % vec_size) == 0;
    const int shared_mem = ((THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31) / 32) * sizeof(float);

    if (aligned) {
        softmax_kernel<vec_size, true><<<batch_size, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
    } else {
        softmax_kernel<1, false><<<batch_size, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

QR is {0,1,3,4,5,9}. Multiply by 10:

0*10=0

1*10=10

3*10=30≡8

4*10=40≡7

5*10=50≡6

9*10=90≡2

So 10QR = {0,10,8,7,6,2}. Then QR + 10QR = {0,1,3,4,5,9} + {0,2,6,7,8,10}. Which is the same as QR + 2QR, which we saw earlier gives all residues. Therefore, QR + 10QR = Z/11Z.

So seems like for any non-zero d, QR + dQR is the entire field. Therefore, for all non-zero a and b, the sumset S(a,b)=Z/11Z, so |S(a,b)|=11. Therefore, for each pair (a,b) with a and b non-zero, there are 11 possible c's (all residues). Hence, the number of triples where a and b are non-zero is 10*10*11=1100. Then, the cases where a or b is zero contribute 121 triples (as calculated earlier). So total is 1100 + 121=1221.

But wait, but let's confirm that there's no overlap. For example, when a=0 and b≠0, the c's are in bQR, which are 6 possibilities. Similarly for b=0. But when a and b are non-zero, c can be anything. So there's no overlap except when a=0, b=0, c=0. Therefore, the total is indeed 121 + 1100=1221.

But wait, the answer seems too straightforward. Let me check again. Suppose a=1 and b=1. Then QR + QR=Z/11Z, as shown earlier, so every c is possible. Similarly for a=2 and b=3, whatever, since their ratio is d=3/2. Since 3/2 is some element in Z/11Z, and since 2 is invertible, d=3/2=3*6=18≡7 mod11. Wait, but regardless of d, the sumset QR + dQR is the entire field, so even if d=7 (which is a non-residue), QR +7QR is the entire field. So in all cases, when a and b are non-zero, the sumset is the entire field, so every c is possible.

Therefore, the conclusion is that:

- When a=0 and b=0: c must be 0. (1 triple)

- When a=0 and b≠0: c can be any of 6 values (bQR), so 10*6=60 triples.

- When b=0 and a≠0: similarly 10*6=60 triples.

- When a≠0 and b≠0: for each of 10*10=100 pairs (a,b), c can be any of 11 values, so 100*11=1100 triples.

Total: 1 + 60 + 60 + 1100= 1221.

But let's check if this is correct. However, is there a case where even when a and b are non-zero, the sumset QR + dQR is not the entire field? For example, if d=0, but d=0 isn't in our case. Since we considered d = b/a with a and b non-zero. But from all the examples I tried, even non-residues for d give the entire field. So perhaps it's a theorem that in Z/pZ for prime p, if QR is the set of quadratic residues including 0, then QR + dQR = Z/pZ for any non-zero d. But I'm not sure if that's a known result. Alternatively, maybe it's specific to p=11. Let me check with another prime. For example, take p=7. QR would be {0,1,2,4}. Let's see if QR + 3QR covers all residues. QR is {0,1,2,4}, 3QR={0,3,6,5} (since 3*1=3, 3*2=6, 3*4=12≡5). Then QR + 3QR would be:

0+0=0

0+3=3

0+6=6

0+5=5

1+0=1

1+3=4

1+6=7≡0

1+5=6

2+0=2

2+3=5

2+6=8≡1

2+5=7≡0

4+0=4

4+3=7≡0

4+6=10≡3

4+5=9≡2

So the sums are 0,1,2,3,4,5,6. So QR +3QR covers all residues mod 7. So in this case, yes. So maybe it's a general property that QR + dQR = Z/pZ when p is an odd prime. If that's the case, then for p=11, the same applies, and so all non-zero a and b would have |S(a,b)|=11. 

Therefore, the answer would be 1221. Let's confirm once more with p=5. QR={0,1,4}. Let's take d=2 (non-residue). 2QR={0,2,3}. QR +2QR:

0+0=0

0+2=2

0+3=3

1+0=1

1+2=3

1+3=4

4+0=4

4+2=6≡1

4+3=7≡2

Thus the sums are 0,1,2,3,4. So QR +2QR=Z/5Z. So yes, again, even for non-residues, the sumset is the entire field.

Hence, it seems like for any prime p, QR + dQR = Z/pZ for any non-zero d. Therefore, in our problem, since 11 is prime, the sumset QR + dQR is Z/11Z for any non-zero d. Hence, for any non-zero a and b, the equation ax² + by² ≡ c mod 11 has a solution for any c. Thus, for each a and b non-zero, there are 11 choices of c. Hence, 10*10*11=1100 triples. Adding the cases where a or b is zero: 1 + 60 + 60=121. Total=1221. Therefore, the answer is 1221.

But just to be 100% sure, let's verify another example. Let’s take p=3. QR={0,1}. Take d=2 (non-residue). QR +2QR={0,1} + {0,2} = {0,1,2,3≡0}. Wait, {0+0=0, 0+2=2, 1+0=1, 1+2=3≡0}. So QR +2QR={0,1,2} which is Z/3Z. So yes, again the entire field. Hence, it's a general result. Therefore, the answer should be 1221, which is 11^3 - 110. Wait, 11^3 is 1331. 1331-110=1221. But that's just a side note. So the final answer is 1221.

**Final Answer**
\boxed{1221}
</think>To determine the number of ordered triples \((a, b, c)\) with \(0 \leq a, b, c \leq 10\) such that there exists \((x, y)\) where \(ax^2 + by^2 \equiv c \pmod{11}\), we will analyze the possible values of \(ax^2 + by^2\) modulo 11.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Quadratic Residues Modulo 11**:
   - The quadratic residues modulo 11 (including 0) are \(\{0, 1, 3, 4, 5, 9\}\).

2. **Cases Involving Zero**:
   - **Case 1: \(a = 0\) and \(b = 0\)**:
     - The equation becomes \(0 + 0 \equiv c \pmod{11}\), which implies \(c = 0\).
     - This gives 1 triple: \((0, 0, 0)\).

   - **Case 2: \(<#sep#>=2:

Let me try using the two-player case for general m and derive an expression.

The idea comes from the fact that in each step, the number of biscuits with each player changes by only 1 net, so the process is a birth-death process. The expected time to reach an absorbing state can be calculated via the formula from the theory of Markov chains.

There is a formula for expected time to absorption starting from state k in birth-death tele sure gauge:

 Suppose the states are 0, 1, ..., m, with 0 and m absorbing.

 For each state i between 1 and m-1 has transition to i-1 with prob p_i and to i+1 with prob q_i.

 The expected time to reach absorbing state is T[i], then:

 T[i] = 1 + p_i *T[i-1] + q_i T[i+1]

 with T[0]=T[m}=0.

 To solve this recurrence rel, there is a standard solution method.

 The general solution for expected time in birth-death process is known.

 According to the formula from the literature (e.g., page 等等):

The expected time T(a) can be computed using the following formula:

 For each state k from 1 to m-1:

 T(a) = \dfrac{2}{m^2 -a^2} sum_{i=1}^a (m^2 - (i)^2)

<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
// Maximum number of floats that can be stored in constant memory
#define MAX_CONST_INPUT_SIZE 16384

// Declare constant memory for input tensor
__constant__ float d_const_input[MAX_CONST_INPUT_SIZE];

// Kernel using constant memory for input data
__global__ void softmax_kernel_const(float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    // Each block processes one row; read data from constant memory
    const float* x_row = d_const_input + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    __shared__ float sdata[THREADS_PER_BLOCK]; // shared memory for reductions

    // 1. Compute maximum value in the row
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = x_row[i];
        max_val = fmaxf(max_val, val);
    }
    sdata[tid] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduction for maximum
    for (int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s /= 2) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = fmaxf(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // 2. Compute exponentials and accumulate sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }
    sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduction for sum
    for (int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s /= 2) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // 3. Normalize the computed exponentials
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

// Fallback kernel using global memory (reference implementation) when input tensor is too large for constant memory
__global__ void softmax_kernel_global(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = x_row[i];
        max_val = fmaxf(max_val, val);
    }
    sdata[tid] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s /= 2) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = fmaxf(sdata[tid], sdata[tid+s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }
    sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    for (int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s /= 2) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid+s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

// Host function to launch the appropriate kernel
void softmax_forward_cuda_const(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    int total_elements = batch_size * num_features;
    int total_bytes = total_elements * sizeof(float);
    
    // Ensure the input tensor fits in constant memory
    TORCH_CHECK(total_elements <= MAX_CONST_INPUT_SIZE, "Input tensor is too large for constant memory kernel");

    // Copy the input tensor to constant memory (device-to-device copy)
    cudaMemcpyToSymbol(d_const_input, x, total_bytes, 0, cudaMemcpyDeviceToDevice);

    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    int shared_mem_size = THREADS_PER_BLOCK * sizeof(float);

    softmax_kernel_const<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(y, num_features);
    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda_const: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

// Main forward function exposed to Python
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    // Input validations
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);
    int total_elements = batch_size * num_features;

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    if (total_elements <= MAX_CONST_INPUT_SIZE) {
        // Use constant memory optimized kernel
        softmax_forward_cuda_const(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);
    } else {
        // Fallback to global memory kernel if input is too large for constant memory
        dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
        dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
        int shared_mem_size = THREADS_PER_BLOCK * sizeof(float);
        softmax_kernel_global<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), num_features);
        cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
        if (err != cudaSuccess) {
            printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda_global: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        }
    }

    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward with constant memory (CUDA)");
}
<#sep#> 11**:
   - The quadratic residues modulo 11 (including 0) are \(\{0, 1, 3, 4, 5, 9\}\).

2. **Cases Involving Zero**:
   - **Case 1: \(a = 0\) and \(b = 0\)**:
     - The equation becomes \(0 + 0 \equiv c \pmod{11}\), which implies \(c = 0\).
     - This gives 1 triple: \((0, 0, 0)\).

   - **Case 2: \(a = 0\) and \(b \neq 0\)**:
     - The equation becomes \(by^2 \equiv c \pmod{11}\).
     - Since \(b \neq 0\), \(by^2\) can take any value in the set \(\{0, b, 3b, 4b, 5b, 9b\}\) modulo 11.
     - There are 6 possible values for \(c\) for each \(b\).
     - There are 10 choices for \(b\) (from 1 to 10).
     - This gives \(10 \times 6 = 60\) triples.

   - **Case 3: \(b = 0\) and \(a \neq 0\)**:
     - The equation becomes \(ax^2 \equiv c \pmod{11}\).
     - Since \(a \neq 0\), \(ax^2\) can take any value in the set \(\{0, a, 3a, 4a, 5a, 9a\}\) modulo 11.
     - There are 6 possible values for \(c\) for each \(a\).
     - There are 10 choices for \(a\) (from 1 to 10).
     - This gives \(10 \times 6 = 60\) triples.

3. **Non-Zero \(a\) and \(b\)**:
   - **Case 4: \(a \neq 0\) and \(b \neq 0\)**:
     - For each pair \((a, b)\) with \(a, b \neq 0\), we need to determine the number of possible values for \(c\).
     - Consider the sumset \(a \cdot \text{QR} + b \cdot \text{QR}\), where \(\text{QR}\) is the set of quadratic residues.
     - Since \(a\) and \(b\) are non-zero, the set \(a \cdot \text{QR} + b \cdot \text{QR}\) covers all residues modulo 11.
     - This is because multiplying by a non-zero element permutes the residues, and the sumset of quadratic residues scaled by any non-zero elements covers all residues.
     - Therefore, for each pair \((a, b)\) with \(a, b \neq 0\), there are 11 possible values for \(c\).
     - There are \(10 \times 10 = 100\) pairs \((a, b)\) with \(a, b \neq 0\).
     - This gives \(100 \times 11 = 1100\) triples.

4. **Total Count**:
   - Summing all cases:
     \[
     1 + 60 + 60 + 1100 = 1221
     \]

Thus, the number of ordered triples \((a, b, c)\) is \(\boxed{1221}\).<#sep#> for T_{k+1}:

T_{k+1}=1 + ((k+1)/m ) T_k + ( (m-k-1)/m ) T_{k+2} 

Now, let me compute T_k — T_{k+1}:

 T_k — T_{k+}= [1 + (k/m) T_{k-1} + (m—k)/m T_{k+} ] — [1 + ... terms ]

等式 becomes:

 T_k - T_{k+1} = (k/m) T_{k-1} — ((k+1)/m) T_k} + terms from T_{k+1}'s T_{k+} and T_{k+2.

<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 512

template <int VEC_SIZE, bool USE_VEC>
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int vec_tid = tid * VEC_SIZE;
    const int vec_stride = blockDim.x * VEC_SIZE;

    __shared__ float sdata[(THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31) / 32];
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Parallel max reduction (coalesced)
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = vec_tid; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
        if (USE_VEC) {
            float4 vec = *reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i);
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, fmaxf(fmaxf(vec.x, vec.y), fmaxf(vec.z, vec.w)));
        } else {
            for (int j = 0; j < VEC_SIZE && (i + j) < num_features; j++) {
                max_val = fmaxf(max_val, x_row[i + j]);
            }
        }
    }

    // Warp-level max reduction
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
        max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));
    if (tid % 32 == 0)
        sdata[tid / 32] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    // Block-level max reduction
    if (tid < 32) {
        max_val = tid < (blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : -INFINITY;
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));
        if (tid == 0)
            sdata[0] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    max_val = sdata[0];

    // Exp + sum reduction
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = vec_tid; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
        if (USE_VEC) {
            float4 vec = *reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i);
            float4 exp_vec = {__expf(vec.x - max_val), __expf(vec.y - max_val),
                             __expf(vec.z - max_val), __expf(vec.w - max_val)};
            *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = exp_vec;
            sum_val += exp_vec.x + exp_vec.y + exp_vec.z + exp_vec.w;
        } else {
            for (int j = 0; j < VEC_SIZE && (i + j) < num_features; j++) {
                float exp_v = __expf(x_row[i + j] - max_val);
                y_row[i + j] = exp_v;
                sum_val += exp_v;
            }
        }
    }

    // Warp-level sum reduction
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
        sum_val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum_val, offset);
    if (tid % 32 == 0)
        sdata[tid / 32] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    // Block-level sum reduction
    if (tid < 32) {
        sum_val = tid < (blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : 0;
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
            sum_val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum_val, offset);
        if (tid == 0)
            sdata[0] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Normalization (coalesced write)
    const float inv_sum = 1.0f / sdata[0];
    for (int i = vec_tid; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
        if (USE_VEC) {
            float4 vec = *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i);
            vec.x *= inv_sum;
            vec.y *= inv_sum;
            vec.z *= inv_sum;
            vec.w *= inv_sum;
            *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = vec;
        } else {
            for (int j = 0; j < VEC_SIZE && (i + j) < num_features; j++)
                y_row[i + j] *= inv_sum;
        }
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    const int vec_size = 4;
    const int aligned_size = (num_features + vec_size - 1) / vec_size;
    const bool can_vec = (aligned_size * vec_size == num_features);
    const int shared_mem = ((THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31) / 32) * sizeof(float);

    if (can_vec) {
        softmax_kernel<vec_size, true>
            <<<batch_size, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
    } else {
        softmax_kernel<vec_size, false>
            <<<batch_size, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

So, yes<#sep#>human: This is an interactive problem.

You are the head coach of a chess club. The club has 2n players, each player has some strength which can be represented by a number, and all those numbers are distinct. The strengths of the players are not known to you.

You need to select n players who would represent your club in the upcoming championship. Naturally, you want to select n players with the highest strengths.

You can organize matches between the players to do that. In every match, you pick two players, they play some games, and you learn which one of the two has higher strength. You can wait for the outcome of a match before deciding who will participate in the next one.

However, you do not want to know exactly how those n players compare between themselves, as that would make the championship itself less intriguing. More formally, you must reach a state where there is exactly one way to choose n players with the highest strengths that is consistent with the outcomes of the matches you organized, but there must be at least two possible orderings of those n players by strength that are consistent with the outcomes of the matches you organized.

Interaction

Your program has to process multiple test cases in one run. First, it should read the integer t (t ≥ 1) — the number of test cases. Then, it should process the test cases one by one.

In each test case, your program should start by reading the integer n (3 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of players to select out of 2n players. The sum of squares of the values of n over all test cases does not exceed 10 000.

Then your program can organize matches zero or more times. To organize a match, your program should print a match description formatted as ? i j — a question mark followed by two distinct numbers of players participating in the match. The players are numbered from 1 to 2n, inclusive. Remember to flush the output after printing the match description. Then your program should read the match outcome — it will be either the greater-than character (>), if the first player in the match description has higher strength, or the less-than character (<), if the second player in the match description has higher strength.

Your program can organize at most 4n^2 matches. After it is done organizing matches, it should print the exclamation mark (!) and continue to the next test case, or exit gracefully if this was the last test case. Remember to flush the output after printing the exclamation mark.

There must be exactly one way to choose n players with the highest strength that is consistent with the outcomes of the matches you organized, but there must be at least two possible orderings of those n players by their strength that are consistent with the outcomes of the matches you organized.

The judging program picks some distinct numbers as the strengths of all players before your program starts organizing matches and uses them to answer the requests.

Example
Input


2
3

&lt;

&lt;

Output

? 3 4

? 4 2

? 5 3

? 6 4

? 3 1

!

Note

In the example, the players in the first test case are sorted by strength in decreasing order. From the matches in the example output, we can deduce that players 1, 2, and 3 have the highest strength, but we do not know how the player 1 compares to the player 2.

    22	    _tmp<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

template <int VEC_SIZE, bool ALIGNED>
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int vec_stride = blockDim.x * VEC_SIZE;
    __shared__ float sdata[(THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31) / 32];

    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Max reduction with vectorized LDG
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid * VEC_SIZE; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i));
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, fmaxf(fmaxf(vec.x, vec.y), fmaxf(vec.z, vec.w)));
        }
    } else {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __ldg(x_row + i));
        }
    }

    // Warp reduction for max
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
        max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));

    if (tid % 32 == 0)
        sdata[tid / 32] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    if (tid < 32) {
        max_val = tid < (blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : -INFINITY;
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
            max_val = fmaxf(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));
        if (tid == 0)
            sdata[0] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    max_val = sdata[0];

    // Sum reduction with vectorized stores
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid * VEC_SIZE; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row + i));
            float4 exp_vec = {__expf(vec.x - max_val), __expf(vec.y - max_val),
                             __expf(vec.z - max_val), __expf(vec.w - max_val)};
            *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = exp_vec;
            sum_val += exp_vec.x + exp_vec.y + exp_vec.z + exp_vec.w;
        }
    } else {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
            float val = __ldg(x_row + i);
            float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
            y_row[i] = exp_val;
            sum_val += exp_val;
        }
    }

    // Warp reduction for sum
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
        sum_val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum_val, offset);

    if (tid % 32 == 0)
        sdata[tid / 32] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    if (tid < 32) {
        sum_val = tid < (blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : 0;
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1)
            sum_val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum_val, offset);
        if (tid == 0)
            sdata[0] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Normalization with vectorized stores
    const float inv_sum = 1.0f / sdata[0];
    if (ALIGNED) {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid * VEC_SIZE; i < num_features; i += vec_stride) {
            float4 vec = *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i);
            vec.x *= inv_sum;
            vec.y *= inv_sum;
            vec.z *= inv_sum;
            vec.w *= inv_sum;
            *reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row + i) = vec;
        }
    } else {
        #pragma unroll 2
        for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x)
            y_row[i] *= inv_sum;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    const int vec_size = 8;
    const bool aligned = (num_features % vec_size) == 0;
    const int shared_mem = ((THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31) / 32) * sizeof(float);

    if (aligned) {
        softmax_kernel<vec_size, true><<<batch_size, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
    } else {
        softmax_kernel<1, false><<<batch_size, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

// Utility function to find the next highest power of 2
inline int next_power_of_two(int x) {
    return 1 << (32 - __builtin_clz(x - 1));
}

template <typename scalar_t>
__global__ void log_softmax_forward_kernel(
    const scalar_t* __restrict__ input,
    scalar_t* __restrict__ output,
    const int dim_size) {

    // Each block handles one batch element (row)
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;

    // Pointers to the input/output row
    const scalar_t* input_row = input + batch_idx * dim_size;
    scalar_t* output_row = output + batch_idx * dim_size;

    // Shared memory for reductions
    extern __shared__ __align__(sizeof(scalar_t)) unsigned char smem[];
    scalar_t* sdata = reinterpret_cast<scalar_t*>(smem);

    // Initialize max_val
    scalar_t max_val = -std::numeric_limits<scalar_t>::infinity();
    // Compute max value
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        scalar_t val = input_row[idx];
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }

    // Reduce max value within block
    sdata[threadIdx.x] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    // Perform tree reduction
    for (unsigned int stride = blockDim.x / 2; stride > 0; stride >>= 1) {
        if (threadIdx.x < stride && threadIdx.x + stride < dim_size) {
            sdata[threadIdx.x] = max(sdata[threadIdx.x], sdata[threadIdx.x + stride]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Compute sum of exp(input - max_val)
    scalar_t sum = 0;
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        scalar_t val = exp(input_row[idx] - max_val);
        output_row[idx] = val;  // Save for reuse
        sum += val;
    }

    // Reduce sum within block
    sdata[threadIdx.x] = sum;
    __syncthreads();

    // Perform tree reduction
    for (unsigned int stride = blockDim.x / 2; stride > 0; stride >>= 1) {
        if (threadIdx.x < stride && threadIdx.x + stride < dim_size) {
            sdata[threadIdx.x] += sdata[threadIdx.x + stride];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    sum = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    scalar_t log_sum = log(sum);

    // Compute output
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        output_row[idx] = (input_row[idx] - max_val) - log_sum;
    }
}

torch::Tensor log_softmax_cuda_forward(torch::Tensor input, int64_t dim) {
    TORCH_CHECK(input.is_cuda(), "input must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(
        input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32 || input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat64,
        "input must be float32 or float64");

    int64_t ndim = input.dim();
    TORCH_CHECK(dim >= -ndim && dim < ndim, "dim out of range");

    dim = dim >= 0 ? dim : dim + ndim;

    // Permute input to bring dim to the last dimension
    std::vector<int64_t> permute_dims;
    for (int64_t i = 0; i < ndim; ++i) {
        if (i != dim) {
            permute_dims.push_back(i);
        }
    }
    permute_dims.push_back(dim);

    input = input.permute(permute_dims).contiguous();
    int64_t batch_size = input.numel() / input.size(-1);
    int64_t dim_size = input.size(-1);

    auto output = torch::empty_like(input);

    // Choose threads as the next highest power of 2 of dim_size, limited to 1024
    int threads = next_power_of_two(dim_size);
    threads = threads < 1024 ? threads : 1024;
    size_t shared_mem_size = threads * sizeof(float);  // Use sizeof(float) as maximum size

    const int blocks = batch_size;

    AT_DISPATCH_FLOATING_TYPES(input.scalar_type(), "log_softmax_forward_cuda", ([&] {
        size_t shared_mem_size = threads * sizeof(scalar_t);
        log_softmax_forward_kernel<scalar_t><<<blocks, threads, shared_mem_size>>>(
            input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
            output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
            dim_size);
    }));

    // Inverse permute to restore original shape
    std::vector<int64_t> inverse_permute_dims(ndim);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < permute_dims.size(); ++i) {
        inverse_permute_dims[permute_dims[i]] = i;
    }
    output = output.permute(inverse_permute_dims);

    return output;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &log_softmax_cuda_forward, "LogSoftmax forward (CUDA)");

</think>To solve the problem, we need to find \(|(a+b)(b+c)(a+c)|\) given the conditions \(ab + bc + ca = 1\) and \((1 + a^2)(1 + b^2)(1 + c^2) = 6923904100\).

First, we expand the product \((1 + a^2)(1 + b^2)(1 + c^2)\):

\[
(1 + a^2)(1 + b^2)(1 + c^2) = 1 + a^2 + b^2 + c^2 + a^2b^2 + b^2c^2 + c^2a^2 + a^2b^2c^2
\]

Using the identity \(a^2 + b^2 + c^2 = (a + b + c)^2 - 2(ab + bc + ca)\) and substituting \(ab + bc + ca = 1\):

\[
a^2 + b^2 + c^2 = (a + b + c)^2 - 2
\]

Next, using the identity \((ab + bc + ca)^2 = a^2b^2 + b^2c^2 + c^2a^2 + 2abc(a + b + c)\) and substituting \(ab + bc + ca = 1\):

\[
a^2b^2 + b^2c^2 + c^2a^2 = 1 - 2abc(a + b + c)
\]

Substituting these into the expanded product, we get:

\[
(1 + a^2)(1 + b^2)(1 + c^2) = 1 + ((a + b + c)^2 - 2) + (1 - 2abc(a + b + c)) + (abc)^2
\]

Simplifying, we find:

\[
(1 + a^2)(1 + b^2)(1 + c^2) = (a + b + c - abc)^2
\]

Given \((1 + a^2)(1 + b^2)(1 + c^2) = 6923904100\), we take the square root:

\[
a + b + c - abc = \pm 83210
\]

We use the identity \((a + b)(b + c)(c + a) = (a + b + c)(ab + bc + ca) - abc\). Substituting \(ab + bc + ca = 1\):

\[
(a + b)(b + c)(c + a) = (a + b + c) - abc
\]

Thus, \((a + b)(b + c)(c + a) = \pm 83210\). Taking the absolute value, we get:

\[
|(a + b)(b + c)(c + a)| = 83210
\]

// Warp-synchronous max
template <typename scalar_t>
__inline__ __device__ scalar_t warp_reduce_max(scalar_t max_val) {
    for (int offset = warpSize / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        max_val = max(max_val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, offset));
    }
    return max_val;
}

// Warp-synchronous sum
template <typename scalar_t>
__inline__ __device__ scalar_t warp_reduce_sum(scalar_t sum) {
    for (int offset = warpSize / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, sum, offset);
    }
    return sum;
}

// Kernel function for computing log softmax
template <typename scalar_t>
__global__ void log_softmax_warp_optimized_kernel(
    const scalar_t* __restrict__ input,
    scalar_t* __restrict__ output,
    const int dim_size) {

    // Each block handles one batch element (row)
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;

    // Pointers to the input/output row
    const scalar_t* input_row = input + batch_idx * dim_size;
    scalar_t* output_row = output + batch_idx * dim_size;

    // Initialize max_val
    scalar_t max_val = -std::numeric_limits<scalar_t>::infinity();
    // Compute max value
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        scalar_t val = input_row[idx];
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }
    
    // Warp-reduce max
    max_val = warp_reduce_max(max_val);
    
    // Broadcast within warp
    max_val = __shfl_sync(0xffffffff, max_val, 0);

    // Compute sum of exp(input - max_val)
    scalar_t sum = 0;
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        scalar_t val = exp(input_row[idx] - max_val);
        output_row[idx] = val;  // Save for reuse
        sum += val;
    }

    // Warp-reduce sum
    sum = warp_reduce_sum(sum);

    // Broadcast sum within warp
    sum = __shfl_sync(0xffffffff, sum, 0);

    scalar_t log_sum = log(sum);

    // Compute output
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        output_row[idx] = (input_row[idx] - max_val) - log_sum;
    }
}

torch::Tensor log_softmax_cuda_forward(torch::Tensor input, int64_t dim) {
    TORCH_CHECK(input.is_cuda(), "input must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(
        input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32 || input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat64,
        "input must be float32 or float64");

    int64_t ndim = input.dim();
    TORCH_CHECK(dim >= -ndim && dim < ndim, "dim out of range");

    dim = dim >= 0 ? dim : dim + ndim;

    // Permute input to bring dim to the last dimension
    std::vector<int64_t> permute_dims;
    for (int64_t i = 0; i < ndim; ++i) {
        if (i != dim) {
            permute_dims.push_back(i);
        }
    }
    permute_dims.push_back(dim);

    input = input.permute(permute_dims).contiguous();
    int64_t batch_size = input.numel() / input.size(-1);
    int64_t dim_size = input.size(-1);

    auto output = torch::empty_like(input);

    // Choose threads as the next highest power of 2 of dim_size, limited to 1024
    int threads = next_power_of_two(dim_size);
    threads = threads < 1024 ? threads : 1024;

    const int blocks = batch_size;

    AT_DISPATCH_FLOATING_TYPES(input.scalar_type(), "log_softmax_warp_optimized", ([&] {
        log_softmax_warp_optimized_kernel<scalar_t><<<blocks, threads>>>(
            input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
            output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
            dim_size);
    }));

    // Inverse permute to restore original shape
    std::vector<int64_t> inverse_permute_dims(ndim);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < permute_dims.size(); ++i) {
        inverse_permute_dims[permute_dims[i]] = i;
    }
    output = output.permute(inverse_permute_dims);

    return output;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &log_softmax_cuda_forward, "LogSoftmax forward (CUDA)");
}

   5.2<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>
#include <limits>

template <typename scalar_t>
__global__ void log_softmax_forward_kernel_2d(
    const scalar_t* __restrict__ input,
    scalar_t* __restrict__ output,
    const int dim_size) {

    // Each block handles one batch element
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int thread_row = threadIdx.y;
    const int thread_col = threadIdx.x;
    const int thread_idx = thread_row * blockDim.x + thread_col;
    const int total_threads = blockDim.x * blockDim.y;

    // Pointers to current batch element
    const scalar_t* input_row = input + batch_idx * dim_size;
    scalar_t* output_row = output + batch_idx * dim_size;

    // Shared memory for reductions
    extern __shared__ __align__(sizeof(scalar_t)) unsigned char smem[];
    scalar_t* sdata = reinterpret_cast<scalar_t*>(smem);

    // Initialize max_val
    scalar_t max_val = -std::numeric_limits<scalar_t>::infinity();
    
    // Compute max value with grid-stride loop
    for (int idx = thread_idx; idx < dim_size; idx += total_threads) {
        max_val = max(max_val, input_row[idx]);
    }

    // Store in shared memory
    sdata[thread_idx] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce within block using 2D structure
    for (int stride = total_threads/2; stride > 0; stride >>= 1) {
        if (thread_idx < stride) {
            sdata[thread_idx] = max(sdata[thread_idx], sdata[thread_idx + stride]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Compute sum of exp(input - max_val)
    scalar_t sum = 0;
    for (int idx = thread_idx; idx < dim_size; idx += total_threads) {
        scalar_t val = exp(input_row[idx] - max_val);
        output_row[idx] = val;  // Store intermediate result
        sum += val;
    }

    // Store partial sums
    sdata[thread_idx] = sum;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce sum within block
    for (int stride = total_threads/2; stride > 0; stride >>= 1) {
        if (thread_idx < stride) {
            sdata[thread_idx] += sdata[thread_idx + stride];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    sum = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    scalar_t log_sum = log(sum);

    // Compute final output with grid-stride loop
    for (int idx = thread_idx; idx < dim_size; idx += total_threads) {
        output_row[idx] = (input_row[idx] - max_val) - log_sum;
    }
}

torch::Tensor log_softmax_cuda_forward(torch::Tensor input, int64_t dim) {
    TORCH_CHECK(input.is_cuda(), "input must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(
        input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32 || input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat64,
        "input must be float32 or float64");

    int64_t ndim = input.dim();
    dim = dim >= 0 ? dim : dim + ndim;

    std::vector<int64_t> permute_dims;
    for (int64_t i = 0; i < ndim; ++i) {
        if (i != dim) permute_dims.push_back(i);
    }
    permute_dims.push_back(dim);

    input = input.permute(permute_dims).contiguous();
    int64_t batch_size = input.numel() / input.size(-1);
    int64_t dim_size = input.size(-1);

    auto output = torch::empty_like(input);

    // Use 32x32 thread block configuration
    dim3 threads(32, 32);
    const int blocks = batch_size;
    const int shared_mem_size = 1024 * sizeof(float);

    AT_DISPATCH_FLOATING_TYPES(input.scalar_type(), "log_softmax_forward_cuda", ([&] {
        log_softmax_forward_kernel_2d<scalar_t><<<blocks, threads, shared_mem_size>>>(
            input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
            output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
            dim_size);
    }));

    std::vector<int64_t> inverse_permute_dims(ndim);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < permute_dims.size(); ++i) {
        inverse_permute_dims[permute_dims[i]] = i;
    }
    output = output.permute(inverse_permute_dims);

    return output;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &log_softmax_cuda_forward, "LogSoftmax forward (CUDA)");

#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>
#include <limits>
#include <cmath>

// Tuned kernel using a compile-time block size parameter for performance tuning
// Supported block sizes: 32, 64, 128, 256, and 512

template <typename scalar_t, int BLOCK_SIZE>
__global__ void tuned_log_softmax_forward_kernel(
    const scalar_t* __restrict__ input,
    scalar_t* __restrict__ output,
    int dim_size) {

    // Each block handles one row (batch element)
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const scalar_t* input_row = input + batch_idx * dim_size;
    scalar_t* output_row = output + batch_idx * dim_size;

    // Shared memory for reductions
    __shared__ scalar_t sdata[BLOCK_SIZE];

    // Phase 1: Compute the maximum value in the row
    scalar_t local_max = -std::numeric_limits<scalar_t>::infinity();
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        scalar_t val = input_row[idx];
        local_max = (val > local_max) ? val : local_max;
    }
    sdata[threadIdx.x] = local_max;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduction to find max value
    for (int stride = BLOCK_SIZE / 2; stride > 0; stride >>= 1) {
        if (threadIdx.x < stride) {
            sdata[threadIdx.x] = (sdata[threadIdx.x] > sdata[threadIdx.x + stride]) ? 
                                   sdata[threadIdx.x] : sdata[threadIdx.x + stride];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    scalar_t max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Phase 2: Compute the sum of exp(x - max_val) for numerical stability
    scalar_t local_sum = 0;
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        // Compute exponentials
        scalar_t exp_val = exp(input_row[idx] - max_val);
        local_sum += exp_val;
    }
    sdata[threadIdx.x] = local_sum;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduction to compute total sum
    for (int stride = BLOCK_SIZE / 2; stride > 0; stride >>= 1) {
        if (threadIdx.x < stride) {
            sdata[threadIdx.x] += sdata[threadIdx.x + stride];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    scalar_t sum = sdata[0];
    scalar_t log_sum = log(sum);
    __syncthreads();

    // Phase 3: Write back the final LogSoftmax values
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < dim_size; idx += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        output_row[idx] = (input_row[idx] - max_val) - log_sum;
    }
}


// Host function
// This function permutes the input so that the reduction occurs on the last dimension,
// selects an optimal block size from the set {32, 64, 128, 256, 512} based on dim_size,
// and then launches the tuned CUDA kernel.

torch::Tensor tuned_log_softmax_cuda_forward(torch::Tensor input, int64_t dim) {
    TORCH_CHECK(input.is_cuda(), "input must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(
        input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32 || input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat64,
        "input must be float32 or float64");

    int64_t ndim = input.dim();
    TORCH_CHECK(dim >= -ndim && dim < ndim, "dim out of range");
    dim = dim >= 0 ? dim : dim + ndim;

    // Permute input so that the target dimension is the last dimension
    std::vector<int64_t> permute_dims;
    for (int64_t i = 0; i < ndim; ++i) {
        if (i != dim) {
            permute_dims.push_back(i);
        }
    }
    permute_dims.push_back(dim);

    input = input.permute(permute_dims).contiguous();
    int64_t batch_size = input.numel() / input.size(-1);
    int64_t dim_size = input.size(-1);

    auto output = torch::empty_like(input);

    // Select an optimal block size from {32, 64, 128, 256, 512}
    int optimal_block_size = 256; // Default value
    if (dim_size <= 32) {
        optimal_block_size = 32;
    } else if (dim_size <= 64) {
        optimal_block_size = 64;
    } else if (dim_size <= 128) {
        optimal_block_size = 128;
    } else if (dim_size <= 256) {
        optimal_block_size = 256;
    } else if (dim_size <= 512) {
        optimal_block_size = 512;
    } else {
        optimal_block_size = 512; // For larger dimensions, cap at 512 threads per block
    }

    const int blocks = batch_size;

    AT_DISPATCH_FLOATING_TYPES(input.scalar_type(), "tuned_log_softmax_forward_cuda", ([&] {
        if (optimal_block_size == 32) {
            tuned_log_softmax_forward_kernel<scalar_t, 32><<<blocks, 32>>>(
                input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                dim_size);
        } else if (optimal_block_size == 64) {
            tuned_log_softmax_forward_kernel<scalar_t, 64><<<blocks, 64>>>(
                input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                dim_size);
        } else if (optimal_block_size == 128) {
            tuned_log_softmax_forward_kernel<scalar_t, 128><<<blocks, 128>>>(
                input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                dim_size);
        } else if (optimal_block_size == 256) {
            tuned_log_softmax_forward_kernel<scalar_t, 256><<<blocks, 256>>>(
                input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                dim_size);
        } else if (optimal_block_size == 512) {
            tuned_log_softmax_forward_kernel<scalar_t, 512><<<blocks, 512>>>(
                input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
                dim_size);
        }
    }));

    // Inverse permutation to restore original data layout
    std::vector<int64_t> inverse_permute_dims(ndim);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < permute_dims.size(); ++i) {
        inverse_permute_dims[permute_dims[i]] = i;
    }
    output = output.permute(inverse_permute_dims);

    return output;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &tuned_log_softmax_cuda_forward, "Tuned LogSoftmax forward (CUDA)");
}

But wait, in the problem statement, n >= k, so for n = k = 2, this works. Now, let's check another case. Let’s take n = 3, k = 2.

So r(3, 2) is to be found such that sum_{i,j=1}^3 1/(x_i + x_j) >= r(3,2) * sum_{i=1}^3 1/(x_i + x_{i+1}), where indices are modulo 3.

First, note that the left-hand side (LHS) has 9 terms: for each pair (i,j), 1/(x_i + x_j). The right-hand side (RHS) has 3 terms: 1/(x₁ + x₂), 1/(x₂ + x₃), 1/(x₃ + x₁).

We need to find the minimal value of (LHS)/(RHS) over all positive x₁, x₂, x₃. Let's attempt to compute it.

Again, perhaps set all variables equal. Let x₁ = x₂ = x₃ = 1. Then LHS has 9 terms each equal to 1/(1 + 1) = 1/2. So LHS = 9*(1/2) = 9/2. RHS is 3*(1/2) = 3/2. So ratio is (9/2)/(3/2) = 3. So LHS/RHS = 3. But maybe there's a case where the ratio is lower.

Suppose we set two variables to 1 and one to a very small ε. Let x₁ = ε, x₂ = 1, x₃ = 1. Then compute LHS and RHS.

Compute LHS: sum_{i,j=1}^3 1/(x_i + x_j).

Break it down:

i=1: j=1: 1/(ε + ε) = 1/(2ε); j=2: 1/(ε + 1); j=3: 1/(ε + 1)

i=2: j=1: 1/(1 + ε); j=2: 1/(1 + 1) = 1/2; j=3: 1/(1 + 1) = 1/2

i=3: j=1: 1/(1 + ε); j=2: 1/(1 + 1) = 1/2; j=3: 1/(1 + 1) = 1/2

So total LHS:

= [1/(2ε) + 2/(1 + ε)] + [1/(1 + ε) + 2*(1/2)] + [1/(1 + ε) + 2*(1/2)]

Simplify:

= 1/(2ε) + 2/(1 + ε) + 1/(1 + ε) + 1 + 1/(1 + ε) + 1

= 1/(2ε) + (2 + 1 + 1)/(1 + ε) + 2

= 1/(2ε) + 4/(1 + ε) + 2

As ε → 0, this behaves like 1/(2ε) + 4/1 + 2 ≈ 1/(2ε) + 6, which tends to infinity.

RHS: sum_{i=1}^3 1/(x_i + x_{i+1}) = 1/(ε + 1) + 1/(1 + 1) + 1/(1 + ε) = 2/(1 + ε) + 1/2

As ε → 0, RHS ≈ 2/1 + 1/2 = 2.5. So the ratio LHS/RHS tends to infinity. Therefore, in this case, the ratio can be made large. So not helpful for finding the minimal ratio.

But perhaps try another configuration where variables are not equal but not approaching zero. Let’s set x₁ = t, x₂ = 1, x₃ = 1, with t varying.

Compute LHS:

sum_{i,j} 1/(x_i + x_j) = [terms where i=1: j=1,2,3: 1/(2t), 1/(t + 1), 1/(t + 1); i=2: j=1,2,3: 1/(1 + t), 1/2, 1/2; i=3: j=1,2,3: 1/(1 + t), 1/2, 1/2]

Total LHS:

= [1/(2t) + 2/(1 + t)] + [1/(1 + t) + 2*(1/2)] + [1/(1 + t) + 2*(1/2)]

= 1/(2t) + 4/(1 + t) + 2

RHS:

1/(t + 1) + 1/2 + 1/(1 + t) = 2/(1 + t) + 1/2

So the ratio is:

[1/(2t) + 4/(1 + t) + 2] / [2/(1 + t) + 1/2]

Let’s compute this. Let’s let’s set s = 1 + t. So t = s - 1, s > 1. Then:

Numerator: 1/(2(s - 1)) + 4/s + 2

Denominator: 2/s + 1/2

This might not simplify easily. Alternatively, try to compute derivative.

If t = 2:

Numerator: 1/4 + 4/3 + 2 ≈ 0.25 + 1.333 + 2 = 3.583

Denominator: 2/3 + 0.5 ≈ 0.666 + 0.5 = 1.166

Ratio ≈ 3.583 / 1.166 ≈ 3.07. So higher than 3.

Ratio ≈ 4.3593 / 1.4524 ≈ 3.001. So still around 3.

This is a bit vague.

The steps are more like:

At any time, the current partitions are A and B.

For each b in B in some order, we process them:

   Check whether there exists any a in A that beats b.

   To do this, compare b against every a in the current A until find such.

   So for each a in current A:

       Ask match between b and a.

       If b was beaten by a, then record the match: b < a

       then break, because we've found the required a. 

       So b can stay in B.

       (No need to check other A's members once we find a valid one)

   So the comparisons for a b: if we find the first a in A that beats b, then OK. 

   else (all a's lose to b):

       Must add b to A, remove any member of A (like the first), to B. 

       Then, we have updated A and B, and must reprocess the new B.

This requires that after each swap, the new members in B (the old A members) are reprocessed again.

Because they might now (depending on other comparisons) have to be in B, but could they be moved back to A again? E.g., the member removed might lose to someone else in B? Not sure.

This seems complicated but doable with code.

However, we have to loop until all B's members are non-top and thus have at least one scaremonger in A they lose to.

This effectively is a greedy selection algorithm akin to finding a maximum set of players where each non-member has at least one member who beats them.

Once this algorithm halts, the A will indeed be the correct subset of top n, because:

- Each member of B has been confirmed to lose to at least one A player.

- Each member of A hasn't been completely defeated by all outside (since the others are in B, which they are in B because they either lost or were swapped out).

Thus this set must be exactly the top n because any larger dominating set would require more than n players.

But why exactly?

Wait the problem states that all players have unique strengths. The true top n players have strength higher than the bottom n. Thus, any bottom player must be beaten by at least one top player, because the top n contain all players stronger than it, and there are n top players. Thus, the minimal dominating set for the bottom is exactly the top players. 

This seems correct.

Now, once the A is determined via this process, which is now exactly the top subset, the next thing is to ensure that their pairwise comparisons are not sufficient to determine their full order.

Because:

The only comparisons between members of A that occurred are:

- When a member of A was compared to some B members to verify they are in A.

- When a member was swapped in, the new member was checked against previous A members and found them all beaten by that member.

Wait for example, when a new member is moved into A after unbeating all current A members, then during that step, that new member would have been compared to each member in the former A to determine their relative strength.

Thus in this process, the outgoing member (the one left for B) is one that was lost to the new member, so the comparison between new member and old holds.

But between other members of A, those may or may not have been compared.

For example, suppose A had Player X and Y. Both were compared to the B player until finding an a that beat it. But X and Y were never compared directly.

Thus, their order between them is unknown, so the problem's condition holds.

Thus, the only comparisons between A members are those required to second-guess whether to swap.

Hmm, for example, if each time a new member comes into A, they have beaten all current members of A (to cause the swap). Thus, for each new member, their comparison with the existing A members is done (all the members are compared against them), but existing members may not have been compared between themselves.

Thus, any pair that was not the reason for a swap would not have their order decided. Thus, this gives an uncompleted order.

Thus this may fulfill the condition.

Now, there remains a possible issue that during the process of finding a suitable a for a b, many comparisons between A members are not done, thus leaving their order ambiguous.

Thus, this algorithm may work.

The steps needed to implement this:

First, track the current A and B sets.

While the process is ongoing:

   For each B member:

       Perform matches compare b with a in A until find some a.

       // collect the found matches

       // Track all match responses.

       ... fn then,

       if found such a:

           mark that this b has been processed.

       else:

           need to move b into A and remove an arbitrary member of A.

           then, after move, the member removed now must be considered in B again.

This requires the algorithm to be implemented in a loop where we cannot just process B players once but possibly multiple times because new members may be added to A and old ones moved to B. Thus the B set must be re-processed in case new members need to be checked.

The algorithm can proceed as follows in code:

Initialize A as first n players.

 B as 2n — first n players.

 Then repeat:

    any change := false

    create a list of all players in B and A.

    For each b in B:

         // find any a in current A such that a beats b.

         // To do so, for each a in A in order:

             result = compare b and a.

             if the function returns that a beats b:

                then we have found a, break.

         If found such a:

                do nothing except log these matches.

         else: 

                // Need to transfer b to A, and move a loser to B.

                // Which a to move? any of the A players, pick first one?

                // pick the first a in A.

                // but to ensure symmetry, choose any (e.g., arbitrary a in A)

                remove a_to_remove from A, add to B

                add b to A.

                // Or the above can be done as:

                newA = A | {b} \ {a_to_remove}

                newB = B \ {b} | {a_to_remove}

                // thus, set A to newA, B to newB.

                any change = True

                // but the B now includes a_to_remove, so needs to be reprocessed.

                // So for the current loop, we may need to restart processing B?

                // perhaps better to break and reloop.

                break processing of this b.

    until no more changes.

Once this loop terminates,'s when for all b in B, there exists an a in current A beats them.

At that point, the current A set is the top n players.

The total matches are O(n^2).

Now, to ensure that the top n's order is not complete, we can see that many comparisons between them are not done.

The total number of required comparisons is indeed O(n²), which is under the problem's limit.

Once the A is settle, the coach must ensure that he stops here and can declare the top n as the set A.

But also needs to ensure that there exist at least two different possible orderings of the top n that are consistent with the matches.

To do that, there must be at least one pair of members in the top with which we haven't decided their order.

To force this, we can have at least one pair that has not been match

But in the current algorithm, perhaps during the process, even if the top set is correctly determined, some pairs of their members might not have been compared. 

Because the only comparisons between them are those done when a new member was added and compared to all existing members of A (when it was being 'tested' to enter).

So, for instance; if the top has members {1,2,3}, and member 3 came into A by beating all of them, then it must have compared with 1 and 2 (so their order is known between the old A members and new).

However, some initial members of A may not have been compared with each other. For example, if 1 and 2 were part of the initial A and never needed to compare against each other since they were never required to compare because during the time other B members were being evaluated against them. So the relative order between 1 and 2 would be unknown.

Thus, in most cases, there are such gaps.

Hence this approach should work.

    11	__<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>
#include <limits>

// Store the dimension size in constant memory for fast read-only access
__constant__ int d_dim_size;

// CUDA kernel using constant memory for dim size
template <typename scalar_t>
__global__ void log_softmax_forward_kernel_const(
    const scalar_t* __restrict__ input,
    scalar_t* __restrict__ output) {

    // Each block handles one batch element (row)
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;

    // Compute pointer offsets using the constant dimension size
    const scalar_t* input_row = input + batch_idx * d_dim_size;
    scalar_t* output_row = output + batch_idx * d_dim_size;

    // Allocate shared memory for reductions
    extern __shared__ __align__(sizeof(scalar_t)) unsigned char smem[];
    scalar_t* sdata = reinterpret_cast<scalar_t*>(smem);

    // Compute the maximum value in the row
    scalar_t max_val = -std::numeric_limits<scalar_t>::infinity();
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < d_dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        scalar_t val = input_row[idx];
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }

    sdata[threadIdx.x] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce maximum value using tree reduction in shared memory
    for (unsigned int stride = blockDim.x / 2; stride > 0; stride >>= 1) {
        if (threadIdx.x < stride && threadIdx.x + stride < d_dim_size) {
            sdata[threadIdx.x] = max(sdata[threadIdx.x], sdata[threadIdx.x + stride]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Compute the sum of exp(input - max_val) in a numerically stable manner
    scalar_t sum = 0;
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < d_dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        scalar_t exp_val = exp(input_row[idx] - max_val);
        // Temporarily store exp value in output for reuse
        output_row[idx] = exp_val;
        sum += exp_val;
    }

    sdata[threadIdx.x] = sum;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce sum in shared memory
    for (unsigned int stride = blockDim.x / 2; stride > 0; stride >>= 1) {
        if (threadIdx.x < stride && threadIdx.x + stride < d_dim_size) {
            sdata[threadIdx.x] += sdata[threadIdx.x + stride];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    sum = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    scalar_t log_sum = log(sum);

    // Final computation of LogSoftmax
    for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < d_dim_size; idx += blockDim.x) {
        output_row[idx] = (input_row[idx] - max_val) - log_sum;
    }
}

// Host function that launches the CUDA kernel
torch::Tensor log_softmax_cuda_forward(torch::Tensor input, int64_t dim) {
    TORCH_CHECK(input.is_cuda(), "input must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(
        input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32 || input.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat64,
        "input must be float32 or float64");

    int64_t ndim = input.dim();
    TORCH_CHECK(dim >= -ndim && dim < ndim, "dim out of range");
    dim = dim >= 0 ? dim : dim + ndim;

    // Permute input to bring the reduce dimension to the last dimension
    std::vector<int64_t> permute_dims;
    for (int64_t i = 0; i < ndim; ++i) {
        if (i != dim) {
            permute_dims.push_back(i);
        }
    }
    permute_dims.push_back(dim);
    input = input.permute(permute_dims).contiguous();

    int64_t batch_size = input.numel() / input.size(-1);
    int64_t dim_size = input.size(-1);

    // Copy the dimension size to constant memory for fast read-only access
    cudaMemcpyToSymbol(d_dim_size, &dim_size, sizeof(int));

    auto output = torch::empty_like(input);

    // Determine the number of threads as the next power of 2 (capped to 1024)
    int threads = 1;
    while (threads < dim_size) threads <<= 1;
    if (threads > 1024) threads = 1024;
    size_t shared_mem_size = threads * sizeof(float);
    int blocks = batch_size;

    AT_DISPATCH_FLOATING_TYPES(input.scalar_type(), "log_softmax_forward_cuda", ([&] {
        size_t smem_size = threads * sizeof(scalar_t);
        log_softmax_forward_kernel_const<scalar_t><<<blocks, threads, smem_size>>>(
            input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),
            output.data_ptr<scalar_t>());
    }));

    // Inverse permute to restore the original tensor shape
    std::vector<int64_t> inverse_permute_dims(ndim);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < permute_dims.size(); ++i) {
        inverse_permute_dims[permute_dims[i]] = i;
    }
    output = output.permute(inverse_permute_dims);
    return output;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &log_softmax_cuda_forward, "LogSoftmax forward (CUDA) with constant memory");
}

Now, considering that the code must be written in C++ (or other language), but as per instructions, need to provide a description thought process.

So, to recap steps to solve the problem:

1. Choose initial top candidates as first n players.

2. For each player in the bottom group (B), compare him to players in the current top group (A), stopping when one A member can beat him (so this B is confirmed in B).

   If the B player beats all in A, swap him into A and kick out some A member (like the first) to B.

3. Repeat until no more changes occur between sets A and B.

4. Finally, declare that the current A set is the top, and there exists multiple orderings as their mutual comparisons are incomplete.

Thus, the algorithm requires O(n²) matches, which is under the problem's limit of 4n².

Thus this approach should work.

Now, some edge cases:

What if all A's initial members are actually the bottom?

Then the process will move many players into A, until finally ~ the top n get into A. Each such move will require a swap, but eventually the A will settle into the correct top subset.

Thus, this approach works.

Thus, implementing this method should solve the problem.

Therefore, the solution involves adaptive comparison of the players to switch between the top and bottom groups until convergence, ensuring the top group has exactly the n best and that their internal comparisons are not complete.
</think>

To solve this problem, we need to determine the top n players out of 2n players such that there's exactly one possible subset of n players identified as the top, but their internal order isn't fully known. The solution must use matches to gather information while keeping within the allowed number of matches (at most 4n² matches).

### Approach
1. **Initial Setup**: Start with an arbitrary partition where the first n players are assumed to be the top (set A), and the remaining n are considered as the potential bottom (set B).
2. **Iterative Relabeling**: For each player in the current bottom set (B), check if it can be beaten by any player in the current top set (A). If a player in B defeats all members of A, it is moved to set A, and the weakest member of the previous set A (arbitrarily the first one) is moved to B.
3. **Termination**: Repeat the process until no more swaps are needed between sets A and B. This ensures that every member of B has an opponent in A who can defeat them, making set A the top n players.
4. **Ensure Ambiguity**: By not requiring all pairwise comparisons among the top players, we ensure their internal order is not fully determined, meeting the problem's second condition.

#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
